<!-- ABOUTME: Enforced by scripts/check-test-matrix.mjs in CI; seeded 2026-07-09 (P0-9) with all FRs, P1–P6, and the §3 named-invariant sweep. -->

# Emend Test Matrix

**Status:** Seeded 2026-07-09 (P0-9). The completeness measure for the rewrite (test-plan §9, TP-Q2): **no numeric coverage gate — the bar is "no FR without a test."** Every functional requirement (`FR-*`, functional-spec §3) and every load-bearing property (`P1–P6`, test-plan §2) has a row here. Downstream units fill in test names against these existing rows as they land (implementation-plan: no unit starts before its references exist here).

**Traces to:** test-plan §9 (completeness measure), §2 (properties P1–P6), §3 (named-invariant sweep). Owning doc for changes to the measure: `docs/test-plan.md`.

**Manual FR-ED-5 positive inference fixture (#2626):** `voice::transcription::whisper_backend::tests::pinned_wav_transcribes_recognizable_jfk_phrase` is `#[ignore]` because the catalog-pinned model and commit-pinned WAV are external downloads. It passed with those verified bytes on macOS; [README](../README.md) gives the command and hashes. It is deliberately excluded from the enforced table: `check-test-matrix.mjs` rejects ignored tests as non-running, and the ordinary gate cannot claim a transcript from unavailable fixture bytes.

| NI-project-routing-rendezvous | TD-20 rulings (5) and (10): every route away is a versioned request/outcome handshake over the injected app-local registry, with one `Focused` / `Opened` / reasoned `Failed` terminal disposition. The absolute deadline is measured from the user's ask; an owner re-reads immediately before work, a request already expired at that read performs nothing, and the requester makes the one recorded final-read tolerance without accepting a late timestamp. Request publication stages in a sanctioned sibling directory outside the watched request lane and holds a persistent staging-lane OS lock through rename; deterministic barriers prove both request scans and acquisition cleanup skip a live publisher, while a later acquirer preserves only the lock sentinel and nonblockingly removes every other inner-lane entry, including canonical and noncanonical files plus nested directories; link metadata is inspected without resolving link targets. Watched-lane artifacts outside the exact `<uuid>.json` allowlist — including an unrecognized stem and a UUID-valid stem with the wrong extension — still fail soft. Malformed/truncated/mismatched records cannot fabricate success or extend a wait. Acquirer cleanup is a positive sanctioned-artifact allowlist; keyed directories for deleted canonical roots are reclaimed from `owner.json` only when the real OS lock is free. Fast tests inject the clock and notification turn with no sleeps; real-process tests take the OS lock, receive through the native watcher, and answer, including a Windows T5 route whose transported roots cover spaces, Unicode, a 248–260 UTF-16-unit project path, and case-equivalent project spellings sharing one registry. Timing varies in-time / expired / ack-race / barriered publication / crash-left staging; content varies valid / corrupt / absent / UUID-valid-wrong-extension; topology varies in-process / genuinely cross-process; paths vary ordinary / spaces / Unicode / near-limit / case-equivalent spelling. | rust | one_request_is_answered_exactly_once, a_receiver_scan_cannot_remove_an_in_progress_request_publication, a_wedged_owner_fails_at_the_deadline_without_leaving_a_request, the_wait_budget_is_measured_from_the_users_ask, an_already_expired_request_is_never_published_or_performed, an_owner_that_reads_an_expired_request_performs_nothing, the_final_read_accepts_an_on_time_ack_even_when_its_notification_arrives_after_expiry, a_late_ack_is_not_mistaken_for_fulfilment, corrupt_request_and_outcome_json_fail_soft_without_extending_the_deadline, an_acquirer_sweep_leaves_only_sanctioned_registry_artifacts, dead_project_registry_sweep_uses_the_recorded_root_and_respects_a_live_lock, a_real_owner_process_takes_the_os_lock_and_answers_the_request, a_real_windows_owner_routes_spaces_unicode_near_limit_and_case_equivalent_spelling | present | P2a-11d (#1748); D26; FR-X-10/11; J9; test-plan §3. `FR-X-11` remains pending only for P2a-11h's complete outcome presentation; P2a-11g now makes the backend outcome stream reachable. **`P` legend (two unrelated namespaces):** `P1`–`P6` (incl. `P4a`/`P4b`) are the **test-plan §2 properties** and appear as row **IDs**. `P0-*`/`P1-*`/`P2a-*`/`P2b-*`/`P3-*`… in the **Notes** column are **implementation-plan phase/unit refs** (the unit that will land the row's tests) — never row IDs.

## How this file is enforced

`scripts/check-test-matrix.mjs` runs in CI (job `test-matrix`) and fails the build when:

1. **Completeness** — a spec FR (`- FR-AREA-n (PRIORITY): …` in functional-spec §3) or a property (`- **Pn …` in test-plan §2) has **no row** here. The scan reads these as **markdown list items**; an FR/property introduced via a heading or table cell instead of a `-` list item is invisible to it — keep new definitions in the list-item form.
2. **Validity** — a row **names a test** (`Test IDs` column) that does **not exist** in the sources for its `Test kind`, or exists only as an `#[ignore]`d / `.skip`ped test (would not run).
3. **Execution** (#1814) — a row names a test that exists but that **nothing ran**. This half needs `--artifacts <dir>`, a directory of the suites' own results documents, and only *enforces* the kinds named by `--require-kinds`; other kinds are reported as warnings. `npm run matrix:check` with no flags runs halves 1–2 only and prints an unmissable banner saying so. The Windows CI execution-check step (`windows-self-hosted.yml`) passes both flags. The maintainer checks that result and hosted CI for the current PR head before merging; GitHub does not mechanically block a merge.

**Format contract (parsed by the script):** every traceability table has the header `ID | Requirement / invariant | Test kind | Test IDs | Status | Notes`.

- **Test kind** — `rust` (unit/proptest), `vitest` (frontend component), `e2e` (Playwright), `node` (node:test tooling), or a non-code kind: `pending` (test not written yet), `manual` (release-gate/live-smoke), `descoped` (cut from v1).
- **Test IDs** — comma-separated concrete identifiers, matched **exactly**: for `rust`, the `fn` name (normally a `#[test]`/`proptest!` test fn — the check locates the `fn` by name and does not itself distinguish a test fn from a plain helper of the same name, so name real assertions only). The **one sanctioned exception** is a test-plan §6 benchmark assertion — a plain `fn` its `harness = false` bench binary's `main` calls — which may be named only by a `present-not-gating` row that says so: `P5-comment-fold-bench` and `P6-delivery-apply-bench` are the live pair; for `vitest`/`e2e`/`node`, the full string title on `it()`/`test()`/`describe()`. Escape a comma inside an identifier as `\,`; unescaped commas separate IDs. `—` means no test named yet (pending rows).
- **Qualifying an identifier with its file** (#2250) — an entry may also be written `<repo-relative-file>::<identifier>`, e.g. `src-tauri/src/store/settings.rs::save_then_load_round_trips`. This is **required** when more than one file in the kind defines that identifier: a bare title cannot say which of them the row means, so the check fails it and lists the candidate files. Where the identifier is unique in its kind — the overwhelming majority — the bare form stays correct and is preferred. A row that genuinely covers the same-named test in two files names both, as two entries. The left side is read as a path only when it ends in one of the kind's own source extensions, so an identifier that merely contains `::` is still an identifier.
- **Status** — `present` (tests exist and run), `present-not-gating`, `partial`, `pending`, `manual`, or `descoped`. A row that names tests is existence-checked regardless of status.
- **One column, two axes.** `Status` carries *both* requirement-coverage completeness (`partial`) and gate-execution (`present` / `present-not-gating`), and it holds one value. A row that is both partly covered and non-gating — `FR-X-4` is the live case — must pick the execution value, because that is the half the checker enforces; the coverage half then survives only in `Notes`. Read a `present-not-gating` row’s `Notes` before concluding its requirement is fully covered.
- **`present-not-gating`** (#1814, ratified by CJ 2026-09-02) — the row's tests exist and are active, but every one of them runs only **outside** the blocking gate: a `@quarantine`-tagged e2e case excluded by `npm run test:e2e:ci` (#1403), or a `tests/perf` benchmark that test-plan §6 defines as tracked and never a PR gate. The execution check enforces the distinction across **four cases, not two**: a `present` row naming a test that did not execute fails; a `present-not-gating` row whose every named test *did* execute fails too — so the status cannot outlive the gap it records; a `present-not-gating` row that mixes an executed test with one that did not fails as a relabelled mixed row rather than reading as honest under either status (CJ's 2026-09-03 #2234 ruling, mechanically enforced by #2284 — see the row-split rule below); and a `present-not-gating` row whose named tests *all* stayed unexecuted passes silently, because nothing in a single run can tell "honestly non-gating" from "the gating test broke too" when the status is row-level. It exempts a row from running, never from existing.
- **A row splits when its tests do not share one answer** (CJ's ruling of 2026-09-03, #2234 — https://github.com/williacj/cowrite/issues/2234#issuecomment-5534426688). `present-not-gating` is **row-level**, so a row naming both gating tests and a test nothing runs is honest under neither status: `present` credits the test that never executes, and `present-not-gating` drops the execution guarantee from every gating test beside it. The remedy is to give the non-gating test its own row naming a subset of the same requirement — `P5`/`P5-comment-fold-bench`, `P6`/`P6-delivery-apply-bench`, and `NI-frontmatter-fold`/`-perf` before them. Split only where the gap is permanent (a `test = false` benchmark); a gap that exists only because one venue cannot run the test is a venue question, not a row shape. Read "keeps its execution guarantee" at the level the gate currently enforces: `vitest` and `e2e` findings fail the Windows CI job, while `rust` and `node` findings are reported as named warnings (test-plan §9, "The gate names its own reach").

**Filling a row in:** when a unit lands its tests, change `Test kind` to the harness, list the `Test IDs`, and set `Status: present`. The check verifies they exist on the merge SHA. Never delete a failing/named test to make the check pass — raise with CJ (CLAUDE.md).

The job-list read only names the cause, so an unreadable count still publishes, under the undetermined cause, rather than leaving the head bare — absent must never read as green. Publishing is best-effort: a Checks refusal is reported and never makes the observer itself the failing signal. The observer never queues for the self-hosted Windows pool it observes. Its `permissions:` block names `contents: read` alongside `actions: read` and `checks: write`, because naming any scope sets the rest to `none` and a checkout that cannot read a private repo would leave the displaced head bare — the very outcome the observer exists to prevent. | NI-mcp-project-open | Binding the MCP server on project open (#413, split of #344, TD-1/§3.5): `agent::mcp::ProjectMcpServer::open` binds a fresh loopback+bearer `LoopbackMcpServer` and serves it on a background thread — a wrong or absent token is rejected (401) before any tool runs on this binding too, a correctly authorized call reaches the real §3.2 tool surface, and two projects opened independently never share a token; `mcp_server()` hands back `agent::claude::McpServer` unchanged into the adapter's own `--mcp-config` builder (schema-valid — no user setup, FR-X-2) for the sibling run-commands unit to inject; dropping the binding ("closing the project") stops the accept loop and closes the bound socket so the old address refuses new connections and the token does not outlive the session, bounded by a per-connection read/write timeout even when a connection stalls mid-request before authorization runs (PR #455 review, Important #2); an accepted stream's `O_NONBLOCK` flag is explicitly cleared before the blocking handler runs, since BSD-derived kernels can inherit it from a nonblocking listener (PR #455 review, Important #1); a connection is read through a **borrow** of its stream, never a duplicated handle, so a `CreateProcess` racing on another thread cannot inherit the live connection and turn its close abortive (the peer would see an RST instead of a clean EOF after a complete response); `Debug` is hand-written to redact the token, on both this type and the `agent::claude::McpServer` value it hands back (PR #455 review, Important #3 — the returned type previously derived `Debug` and leaked the token) (test-plan §3 token-leakage sweep) | rust | a_wrong_or_absent_token_is_rejected_on_a_freshly_opened_project, open_binds_loopback_only_with_a_fresh_high_entropy_token_per_project, a_correctly_authorized_call_reaches_the_real_tool_surface, mcp_server_feeds_the_claude_adapters_mcp_config_injection_unchanged, closing_the_project_unbinds_the_server_and_the_old_address_stops_accepting, src-tauri/tests/project_mcp_server.rs::debug_formatting_never_prints_the_bearer_token , a_stalled_connection_does_not_hang_project_close, no_socket_is_read_through_a_duplicated_handle, the_file_lease_probe_does_not_exempt_other_handle_clones | present | #413 (split of #344, superseding its "MCP server startup" third); `emend_lib::agent::mcp::ProjectMcpServer`, integration `src-tauri/tests/project_mcp_server.rs`; the CLI-spawn wiring itself (actually invoking `agent::session::AgentSession::spawn` with this binding's `mcp_server()`) is the sibling run-commands card, not this one. Reviewer-flagged fixes (PR #455 review, review round 2): cross-platform accept-loop nonblocking normalization, a bounded stream timeout so `Drop` can't hang project close, and closing the `Debug`-redaction gap on the returned `McpServer` type. Issue #472 closes the "sibling run-commands card" hand-off this note points at — see `NI-mcp-run-injection`. Windows self-hosted-runner flake fix: `read_request` had been reading through `BufReader::new(stream.try_clone()?)`. On Windows the duplicated socket handle is briefly inheritable, and Rust's `Command` always calls `CreateProcess` with `bInheritHandles: TRUE` — so a run spawning an agent CLI while the server served a connection could leak that connection into the child, leaving the server's `closesocket` no longer the last reference; when the child exited the kernel closed the socket abortively and the peer got an RST after an already-complete response (measured ~1-3 per 1000 requests under concurrent spawning; surfaced as `agent_run_mcp_injection` failing ~20% of runs at `common::mcp_call`, on whichever test happened to race). Fixed by borrowing the stream (`impl Read for &TcpStream`) in all three hand-rolled HTTP endpoints — `agent::mcp`, `crates/test-driver`, `crates/fake-agent` — and pinned workspace-wide by `no_socket_is_read_through_a_duplicated_handle` (`src-tauri/tests/socket_handles.rs`), a static guard rather than a stress test because the defect is probabilistic and platform-specific. #2415 permits one exact fully qualified File-only lease-test helper; extra calls, socket-typed near-matches, other paths and duplicate helpers remain rejected |
| NI-mcp-run-injection | A spawned run is auto-wired to the Emend tool surface (#472, TD-1 "the connection config is injected into the spawned CLI's environment/config so no user setup is required"; FR-X-2): a run takes its MCP server from the **open project** (TD-17 Option B — `NI-open-project` covers the aggregate itself), so every run against that project carries the same address and token with no port/thread/credential churn per turn, product routing never replaces it, and close is refused while a run is active, preserving the live binding and D21 watchdog through the turn. `commands::agent::claude_invocation` takes the bound server **by value, not `Option`** — the launch always carries Emend connection configuration — and a run with no open project to draw one from, or against a root that is not the open project's, surfaces as the run's `Failed` phase (`SessionError::Spawn`) rather than a silently tool-less run (D21, FR-X-3). End-to-end: a **real spawned `fake-agent` binary** consumes the exact `--mcp-config` the production argv builder produced, reaches the real §3.2 tool surface over loopback JSON-RPC carrying the bearer token, and gets back the project's actual content at its actual revision — asserted against what only the real server can answer, so a silent fall-back to the in-process `StubToolServer` fails rather than passes; a tampered `Authorization` header is refused (401) by that same live path | rust | claude_invocation_injects_the_bound_servers_mcp_config, a_run_takes_the_open_projects_bound_server, The legacy single-tab callback surface remains for ordinary dirty guards; the production editor uses `onCoordinatedClose`, which prevents native close before any asynchronous work and requires its final canonical receipt/proof before destruction. #2327 splits the destroy from the session write: the write-failure dialog now describes only a failed write, and a refused close — from the finalization path or from `Quit anyway` — reports its own reason through the app status surface instead of an unattributed rejection. #2334 adds the focus half CJ ruled at UXR-Q20.4: both refused-close sites return the author to the position recorded before the attempt — the destination cancelling uses — because a refused close resolves the attempt with nothing carried out. The finalization site requests it through a ref the freeze-lifting effect consumes, since the pane is `inert` until React commits that render; the `Quit anyway` site restores directly, having no freeze to wait for. Coverage limit (#2345, CJ 2026-10-04): the tests prove the finalization restore request and destination, but jsdom does not enforce `inert`, so they cannot distinguish the effect from an early microtask. D23 has `isTauri()` false and cannot enter the native refused-close path. The effect-after-unfreeze timing remains unexecuted in a runtime that enforces `inert`; a future native-runtime test is needed to prove it. Each cell pins its own site: dropping the finalization request reddens only the first, dropping the `Quit anyway` restore only the second. The queue’s closes are carried out by the window going away, so a window that stays open has carried out none of them; the finalization path therefore re-writes the live session through the ordinary lane, which is what keeps the next ordinary write from resurrecting a discarded untitled document with nobody asked. The cell proves that write independently of the message: with it removed, the last write on the lane is still the close payload under `require_untitled_lossless`. `NI-window-lifecycle-acl` covers the ACL grant that destroy needs, which these cells mock. The compensating write is gated on the same delta the attempt already computed, so the no-write proof path still writes nothing (J2 “it does not write again”), and on the close-attempt generation, so a superseded attempt cannot write behind a newer one. Its receipt→baseline restore is pinned by consequence rather than by inspection: a second attempt after a refused close must write, because a baseline left describing the closed window would make it prove an empty session the backend refuses (#2328 review, finding 3). |

`src/editor/TabbedEditor.tsx`. **#1393 (intermittent session restore):** the tab-set persist effect (`saveTabSession`/`store::tab_session`) fires unconditionally on every mount, React's ordinary semantics — including a fresh project's very first mount, where the sibling restore effect has *already* synchronously pointed `rootRef` at the new root (before its own `await loadTabSession`) while `tabs` in this effect's closure is still empty. Unguarded, that spurious pass wrote an empty session that could race — and beat — the restore's own read, silently clobbering the very session about to be restored (losing one or all open tabs depending on which round trip landed first). The fix pauses every producer of `saveTabSession` while that read is pending, leaving the effect's D21 protected-tab feed and owner notifications live, and clears the pending marker on both success and failure so a read error cannot disable future persistence. Tab-set snapshots and #1379's debounced/blur/quit untitled snapshots share one promise chain (separate from `setOpenTabs`'s existing `tabFeedRef`); each enqueue captures its project root and complete payload before waiting, so neither an unrelated slow write nor a later project/tab change can overtake or retarget an older snapshot. Reproduced pre-fix at 3/4 and 3/6 local failures of `e2e/acceptance.spec.mjs:661` (`-g "open two tabs"`, single worker); 6/6 plus a further 9/9 (`-g "P2a-6 tabs"`) passed post-fix. |

| NI-project-files-recreate-confinement | TD-18 offers Recreate only when the platform/filesystem adapter proves transaction-long confinement. macOS and unsupported adapters return `ConfinementUnavailable` before content read or filesystem write. Windows qualifies only while root, ancestor, parent, and temp handles omit `FILE_SHARE_DELETE`, reparse traversal is refused, and the whole chain remains held through atomic no-replace publication that leaves the target without `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_TEMPORARY`; pre-existing conflicting `DELETE` access fails capability acquisition before content/temp/target I/O. `EEXIST` returns status without reading or overwriting the target; capability loss, cancellation, and every error preserve the Missing buffer/state; concurrent creators yield exactly one `Created`. At the frontend, a completed generation that no longer lists an open document's path is what enters J2's Missing-on-disk lifecycle — but only where that generation actually enumerated the place the document lives: a row it listed without descending (a directory whose read failed, a symlink, an `other`/`unknown` entry) excuses every document beneath it, while a folder that is absent altogether is exactly the deletion evidence that does not. A path hidden before it produces any row is excused only when the backend visibility query says that exact document path cannot be enumerated; an ordinary absent path remains Missing. Every one of those cells except the deleted-folder one is proved by a named one-line mutation at file scope (drop the undescended guard; count clean directories as undescended; count file rows as undescended; excuse the row's own path) — the deleted-folder cell discriminates against a *design* alternative ("require every ancestor to be listed") rather than a one-line change, and is recorded as the boundary it documents; `App`'s drives the assembled shell. `e2e/acceptance.spec.mjs` drives a real deletion on real disk through the real backend (buffer kept, row gone, no write while missing, Keep editing/status/Close…, then identical reappearance clearing the state and resuming autosave). #1516 closes the platform execution gap: exact-head Windows D23 cases drive both `Created` and `Reappeared` through the visible Recreate action and real backend, while the existing macOS case keeps the explicit absence assertion; its cross-platform Save As case drives success and all three no-change exits, recorded separately in `NI-project-files-recreate-save-as-e2e`. #1513 adds the D23 project-files event bridge: a real native write now produces an announced generation that the production listener applies without a `[Refresh]` click, carrying Rust's own notice serialization and event-name constant through the driver envelope. #1444 closes that no-row visibility gap without mirroring the hidden-name list into TypeScript: `project_path_is_enumerable` delegates every ancestor and leaf to `is_user_visible`, the thin Tauri/D23 query reaches that classifier, and every #1446 reconciliation fences its asynchronous answers before entering Missing. A current query failure reports through FR-X-5 while keeping every candidate path Missing, so autosave stays paused rather than recreating a possibly deleted file. Its vitest regressions prove both a deliberately opened `node_modules/pkg/README.md` and a restored hidden tab evaluated against an earlier completed generation stay present, while an ordinary deletion still enters Missing; the D23 browser acceptance drives the hidden path through the real backend query and confirms autosave continues. #1515 retires this row's former reviewer-mode recovery gap: `NI-reviewer-mode-author-surface-boundary` first reaches this real author Missing lifecycle, then proves the whole author workspace and every recovery/guard control unmount on both platform variants. |

against the live document by diffing `base` against `live` (recovering "what changed concurrently" without the caller tracking an edit log) and exact-mapping each proposal edit's anchor through that batch (P1-4's `Anchor::map_through`, reused as-is — no fuzzy re-anchor, per TD-11's note that this partition is whole-document/safe by construction): an untouched anchor applies, rebased to its live position; a touched one is genuinely conflicted and rejected. Decisions route through `apply::Grouping` (as `apply.rs` requires) so one stale group member rejects the whole group (P4b's TD-7 half). One function call returns the fully-spliced text alongside the partition — no observable intermediate state (FR-AGENT-6). ## §3 named-invariant sweep (seeded per test-plan §3)

| ID | Requirement / invariant | Test kind | Test IDs | Status | Notes |
|----|-------------------------|-----------|----------|--------|-------|
| NI-undo-policy | deliveries `addToHistory:false`; accept/reject single undoable; undo-accept-inside-group; user-edit-inside-pending | vitest | undo restores both the text AND the sidecar attribution state — never desynced (finding M9), undo-accept-inside-group: undoing a group decision restores every member to the live set — not just the text, is a single history entry — one Ctrl+Z reverses exactly one decision, a group decision is still one undo entry, replaces the document with the delivered content and reseeds the tracked-change snapshot, dispatches with addToHistory:false — a bare Ctrl/Cmd+Z immediately after a delivery leaves the delivered content in place, does not itself add a step to the undo history — undoDepth never grows from a delivery alone, deliveryTransaction is a pure builder that dispatches nothing and yields the delivered text, leaves the caret where the user left it when the delivery lands after it, keeps a selection range intact across a delivery that lands elsewhere, never asks CM6 to scroll — the delivery transaction carries no scrollIntoView, does not steal focus — an unfocused editor stays unfocused through a delivery, touches only the changed span so untouched text is never rewritten under the user, still delivers correctly when the change is at the very start or end of the document, delivers a document that shares nothing with the live text, never splits a surrogate pair when trimming to the changed span | present | P2a-21 (#156): `editor/trackedChanges.ts` registers `invertedEffects` (`@codemirror/commands`) so a decision's `dropTrackedChanges` is undoable in both directions — `restoreTrackedChanges` re-adds the exact pre-decision `LiveChange`(s) to the live set on undo, and inverting *that* transaction (CM6 re-inverts the popped undo to build the matching redo entry) produces `dropTrackedChanges` again, so redo removes it once more in lockstep with the text. Text and the sidecar attribution snapshot can never desync across an undo/redo cycle. `editor/delivery.ts` (new) is the agent-delivery transaction policy half (TD §3.3 finding M9): `deliveryTransaction` replaces the whole document and reseeds tracked changes tagged `Transaction.addToHistory.of(false)`, so a bare Ctrl/Cmd+Z can never pop it — `applyAgentDelivery` is the dispatch entry point a future session/event listener calls once that live-delivery wiring lands (nothing subscribes to a delivery yet; this module is the transaction-shape contract). #346 makes it that minimal-edit delivery, because UX Principle 2 requires it: the delivered text is still the backend's whole rebased document (TD-2), but it is dispatched as the *changed span only* (`narrowToChangedSpan` — shared prefix/suffix trimmed off, surrogate-pair-safely). A blanket `{from:0,to:doc.length}` replace maps every downstream position onto the end of the replacement, so a delivery landing mid-keystroke teleported the caret to the bottom of the file; the tests here now pin caret, selection, scroll (`scrollIntoView` absent) and focus as untouched — J1 Phase B's "the editor does not jump" asserted rather than assumed. It also retires the old full-replace caveat about collapsed undo-history entries. "user-edit-inside-pending" is P2a-5's pre-existing `editor/trackedChanges.test.ts` test (title has a comma so isn't itself listable here — see "an edit inside a pending change wins (J1)" describe block), re-cited as the invariant's other half. |
| NI-spellcheck-suppression | spellcheck suppressed in markup/frontmatter ranges | vitest | marks the CM6 content editable as spellchecked so the webview's native checker runs over it, renders the suppressed ranges as real spellcheck-false elements in the content DOM, suppresses an insertion's opening and closing delimiters, suppresses a deletion's opening and closing delimiters, suppresses a substitution's arrow separator alongside its braces, suppresses the delimiters of every change in a document holding several, leaves the surrounding prose unsuppressed — only the scaffolding is excluded, keeps an insertion's inserted prose spellchecked — it is text the user will accept, keeps a deletion's removed prose spellchecked — it is still document text until rejected, keeps both sides of a substitution spellchecked while suppressing the arrow between them, suppresses the whole frontmatter block so its keys are never flagged as misspellings, still spellchecks the prose body below the closing frontmatter delimiter, treats a document whose first line is not a fence as having no frontmatter, suppresses a frontmatter block closed by the alternate ... delimiter, suppresses frontmatter and CriticMarkup delimiters together in one document, shifts a change's suppressed delimiters when text is inserted before it, keeps suppressing the delimiters after the user edits inside the pending change, shifts frontmatter suppression when a line is added inside the block, does not rebuild the suppression set on a cursor move that changes nothing it reads, rebuilds the suppression set when a document edit moves the regions, rebuilds the suppression set when a fresh parse seeds new changes, leaves the document byte-identical through mount seeding and suppression rebuilds | present | P2b-9a (#361): `editor/spellcheck.ts` — webview-native spellcheck (§3.1's named mechanism: `EditorView.contentAttributes` `spellcheck="true"` over CM6 content, no vendored dictionary) minus a suppression layer that excludes two range kinds via `spellcheck="false"` mark decorations. There is no CM6-native range-suppression API, so the HTML attribute on a mark-rendered `<span>` is the only lever. **Unexecuted load-bearing assumption:** that a webview resolves `spellcheck` per-node, honouring `false` nested inside a `true` editing host. Inferred from the HTML Living Standard (nearest explicit ancestor wins) and Blink's walk-up resolution, *not* observed — jsdom has no spellchecker and spelling markers are compositor-painted, so neither vitest nor Playwright can assert it; what the tests do pin is that CM6 emits the attribute on a real span. Manual confirmation on a real webview was attempted for #361 and blocked (dev machine has no spellchecker — unregistered Windows spellcheck COM class, no Hunspell dictionary — so the negative control was dead too); still owed on WebView2 and WKWebView. See `docs/journal.md`. Both region sources are canonical, never a frontend regex: CriticMarkup delimiters come from `delimiterSpans` over `trackedChangesField` (i.e. `textops::critic`'s parse, TD-13, mapped forward by stable identity — so suppression *follows* edits for free), and frontmatter from `structuralDecorations`'s shared `frontmatterEnd` rule (both helpers exported here rather than re-implemented, so the "where does frontmatter end" / "which bytes are scaffolding" predicates each keep one home). **The named boundary:** a change's *content* (an insertion's inserted prose, a deletion's still-present text, both sides of a substitution) stays spellchecked — it is real prose the user will accept or keep; only the `{++`/`--}`/`~>` scaffolding is suppressed. That falls out of `delimiterSpans` being the exact complement of the inner segments. Built as a viewport-scoped `ViewPlugin` (the #216 pattern), and unlike the structural layer it does *not* depend on the active line — suppression is identical whether a delimiter is collapsed or revealed — so the caret-move keystroke path skips the rebuild entirely. |
| NI-keymap-platform | keymap module per-platform (Cmd/Ctrl); J1 triage keys | e2e | keyboard-only triage: platform Mod+Enter accepts and Mod+Backspace rejects (FR-X-1) | present | P2a-5 (#155) lands the platform-aware J1 triage-key e2e half: `editor/trackedChanges.ts`'s `redlineKeymap` binds `Mod-Enter` (accept) / `Mod-Backspace` (reject), CodeMirror resolving `Mod` to Cmd on macOS and Ctrl on Windows/Linux per FR-X-1 (`e2e/acceptance.spec.mjs`; also unit-covered in `editor/trackedChanges` "keyboard triage (platform-aware Mod)"). P2b-9b (#362) lands the dedicated per-platform keymap *module* this row's note flagged as outstanding: `editor/keymap.ts` — see `NI-keymap-module` for its own tests. |
| NI-keymap-module | single keymap module (P2b-9b, #362): one home for the app's Cmd/Ctrl bindings, per-platform Mod resolution + UI-facing display text, and a no-duplicate-definition contract so a binding is never restated in a second place | vitest | names every currently-shipped J1 triage\, save\, and J14 format binding exactly once, resolves Mod to Cmd on macOS, resolves Mod to Ctrl on Windows/Linux, leaves non-Mod parts of the spec untouched, renders the mac symbol cluster with no separator, renders the Windows/Linux name joined with +, ArrowRight/ArrowDown move to the next tab (wrapping past the last), ArrowLeft/ArrowUp move to the previous tab (wrapping before the first), Home/End jump to the first/last tab, Enter/Space activate the currently-focused index, returns undefined for a key it does not handle, matches a raw Mod-key CM6 binding (the original shape), matches a platform-hardcoded binding — the worse FR-X-1 violation not just Mod-, does not match keymap.ts's own spec: shape, no file outside keymap.ts defines a raw or platform-hardcoded CM6 binding | present | P2b-9b (#362): `editor/keymap.ts` is the single source of truth for the app's keybindings — `KEYMAP` names the J1 triage (`acceptChange`/`rejectChange`) and `save` bindings by `spec`/`label` once; `editor/trackedChanges.ts`'s `redlineKeymap` and `editor/EditorShell.tsx`'s `Mod-s` `keymap.of(...)` entry both build their CM6 `KeyBinding` from this table's `spec` rather than restating the key combination (CodeMirror's own keymap facility still performs the actual per-platform `Mod` resolution for these, exactly as before — a pure consolidation, no behaviour change). `resolveMod`/`displayShortcut` expose the resolved binding (`⌘Enter`/`Ctrl+Enter`) for UI that wants to show the real bound key instead of a hardcoded glyph. `tabListNavigation` is the tab strip's roving-tabindex key→target-index table (T2/FR-ED-3), moved out of `editor/TabBar.tsx`'s inline `switch` into this one module alongside the Mod-key bindings — `TabBar.tsx`'s own `NI-tabbar-keyboard` suite is unchanged and still green, since the externally observable behaviour is identical. The "no file outside keymap.ts defines a raw or platform-hardcoded CM6 binding" test is the acceptance criterion's "grep should find no second place defining a binding" enforced in CI rather than by hand: it scans every `src/**/*.ts(x)` file (excluding tests) for the `key: "Mod-…"` `KeyBinding` shape *and* the platform-hardcoded kind (`Cmd-`/`Ctrl-`/`Alt-`/`Shift-`) — the worse FR-X-1 violation, and the one a `Mod-`-only pattern would miss — failing if anything but `keymap.ts` itself is caught defining one; the three pattern-level tests pin that widened match (and its non-match against `keymap.ts`'s own `spec:` shape) directly, independent of what happens to live in the tree today. **Update (#697):** the native menu (Tauri `menu.rs`) now carries its first custom accelerator — `File > Open…`'s `CmdOrCtrl+O` — but as a muda accelerator string on the Rust side, not sourced from this table: `KEYMAP` is a CM6 `KeyBinding` seam (this row's own scanner only reaches `src/**/*.ts(x)`), and nothing today bridges a Rust menu accelerator to a TS keymap spec across the IPC boundary, so there is no existing mechanism this card could have read from without inventing one out of scope for a small stopgap card (see `FR-X-1`'s `is_open_document_event` row). The "cannot drift" guarantee this row establishes therefore still covers only the editor's own CM6 bindings; a second native-menu accelerator should at minimum not restate `CmdOrCtrl+O` a second time (menu.rs's own `OPEN_DOCUMENT_MENU_ID`/accelerator pairing is the only definition), and unifying the two tables (if a Save/accept/reject menu entry is ever added) is a follow-up, not settled here. |
| NI-menu-open-document | `stores/menu.ts` (#697) — the frontend end of the `File > Open…` menu bridge: subscribing to the backend's menu-fired signal, raising the native markdown file picker, and converting the chosen absolute path to the project-relative spelling `OpenRequest` needs | vitest | listens on the open-document menu event and calls the handler with no payload, opens the native picker at the project root with a markdown filter and returns the selected path, src/stores/menu.test.ts::returns null when the user cancels the dialog , src/stores/menu.test.ts::returns null outside a Tauri webview without touching the dialog plugin , strips the project root and a forward-slash separator, strips a trailing separator on the root before comparing, normalizes a Windows backslash separator to the project's forward-slash spelling, refuses a path outside the project root, refuses a sibling directory whose name merely starts with the root's (no separator boundary), refuses the root path itself — there is no relative document to name | present | #697/#1827: `listenForOpenDocumentRequests` delegates to the shared native-or-driver production event pump; `pickDocumentFile` mirrors `onboarding/ipc.ts`'s `pickProjectFolder` precedent with a markdown `add_filter`-equivalent (`filters: [{name: "Markdown", extensions: ["md"]}]`) in place of `directory: true`, including the identical Tauri-only/cancel-is-`null` degrade. **Review of PR #718 (Important):** `pickDocumentFile` now takes the open project's `root` and passes it as the dialog's `defaultPath`; without it the picker opened wherever the OS last left the app (Documents/home on first run) and invited a pick `relativeToProjectRoot` would then silently refuse — pointing the picker at `root` is what makes that hard refusal livable rather than a trap for a directory the app never suggested. `relativeToProjectRoot` is the one pure function in the module — no Tauri mocking needed to test it — and is the seam the `FR-ED-3` row's "refuses a path chosen outside the open project root" case is built on: sidecar identity is project-root-relative (`store::service::tracked_paths`), so a path that cannot be expressed relative to the open root is refused (`null`) rather than opened under a spelling `commands::path_confine::confine` would later reject anyway. Accepts either OS separator in the input (whichever one the picker that produced both paths actually used) and always emits the app's canonical forward-slash spelling, matching `commands::path_confine::canonical_relative`'s identical normalization on the Rust side. |
| NI-settings-migration | Settings persistence, schema-v2 migration, and cross-process patch publication. Fresh/default and migrated v1 settings make reveal-at-cursor false while preserving every pre-existing field. A field-scoped patch holds a companion cross-process lock across latest-load → patch → atomic save, so unrelated concurrent changes survive. The app-scoped service watches before reading, retains its complete last-good snapshot across invalid input, publishes local and external changes, and equality-deduplicates a writer echo. Before any valid snapshot exists, a malformed or unreadable file remains an honest `get_settings` error; Settings UI Retry, a later valid watched replacement, or a successful patch can recover. P2b-1b (#348) closes the `partial` note above: `timeline/collapse.ts`'s pure predicates (`isThreadCollapsedByDefault`/`isChangeSetCollapsedByDefault` — the "fully decided, not any decision" boundary) drive a single-line collapsed render in `CommentThreadCard`/`ChangeSetCard`, wrapped in the same `TimelineCard` shell and toggleable in place ([Expand]/[Collapse]) via component-local view state, never persisted; each card reports its effective collapsed value up through `onCollapsedChange` (`timeline/CommentCard.tsx` passes it straight through) into `TimelinePanel`'s `effectiveCollapsedIds` map, which `itemHeightFor` reads to pick each row's `VirtualList` height — falling back to the same data-driven default for any card id never yet mounted (an off-screen rehydrated resolved thread sizes correctly without ever having been on screen). `onCollapsedChange` reports alongside it whether that value is the user's *explicit* choice (a click) or merely what the collapse rule produced, and only explicit choices land in the separate `pinnedCollapsedIds` map that seeds a card's `initialCollapsed` on remount: the two answer different questions, and seeding from the effective value instead converted every collapse-by-default card into a pinned one after a single scroll out of and back into the window, so the default rule never fired again (PR #481 review round 2). A live-run `agent-working` card (#477) flows through the same virtualized stream and reaches `isCardCollapsedByDefault`, but deliberately carries no collapse rule of its own — it falls through to the lenient `default` branch and is never collapsed, since a run in progress has nothing decided to collapse away (pinned rather than left to the fallback implicitly, PR #481 rebase round). `VirtualList`'s `itemHeight` prop now also accepts a per-item height function; `virtualization.ts` gained `cumulativeOffsets`/`computeVisibleRangeVariable`/`totalContentHeightVariable`, a variable-height generalization of the existing fixed-height windowing math (still fully known ahead of render, never DOM-measured — per-card *dynamic* height stays P2b-10's job) so a collapsed row's shorter height is what the windowing/spacer/facet-jump math actually uses, preventing the scroll jump a uniform-height assumption would cause. A change-set with zero changes never collapses by this rule (no "every change decided" to have reached — `Array.prototype.every`'s vacuous truth on an empty array would otherwise wrongly collapse a placeholder/malformed card, caught by a regression in `TimelinePanel.facets.test.tsx`'s 5k-card window-size assertion during this card's own work). PR #392 review round: `VirtualList`'s facet-jump scroll target is clamped to the container's actual max scroll (state/DOM could disagree past the end of content — jsdom doesn't clamp/emit a scroll event the way a real browser does, so the original tests were green for a reason that didn't hold in production); submitting a message while a non-Chat facet is active now restores All so the new card is no longer filtered out (FR-X-5, UX Principle 7). Round 3: the clamp is now deferred until the container has actually been measured, so a `scrollRequest` passed at mount by any consumer of the exported `VirtualList` (not just `TimelinePanel`) is clamped rather than applied against `containerHeight === 0`; covered by direct `VirtualList.test.tsx` tests, which also pin both halves of the request-identity contract: a later re-measure or append never re-scrolls the user (Principle 2), and a *fresh* request object carrying the same index does jump again — so a repeat facet click still works (round 4, PR #392). The submit path's guarantee is scoped to 'no longer filtered out' — it deliberately does not scroll to the new card, since auto-scroll on append is pinned against by `TimelinePanel.test.tsx` under Principle 2 and ux-design §4 does not say whether a user's own submit is exempt (raised on PR #392). P2b-5b (#355): a finding is never a separate card `kind` (D6 — the same `CommentThreadCard` renders every comment provenance), so the Findings facet was reworked to select on the thread's own `severity`/`skill` fields (`Facet.match`) instead of the placeholder `"finding"` kind literal the P2b-1a card had reserved — that literal, and the tests pinning it as a stub, are retired here. P2b-1c (#449, UXR-Q2 — ux-design §5, amending §1 Principle 2/§4 "Timeline at scale"): drops the PR #392 facet-restore-on-submit `TimelinePanel.handleSubmit` shipped, replacing it with the ratified submit story — a submit never changes the active facet; it reuses the identical `scrollRequest` seam facet-jump already uses to scroll to the user's own card when the (unchanged) active facet includes it, since the card is always the freshly appended most-recent one; when the facet excludes it, `timeline/NewInFacetAffordance.tsx` (new) renders the `N new in <Facet> →` affordance instead of silently dropping the card from view (FR-X-5), naming the facet `timeline/facets.ts`'s new `facetForCard` resolves for the card's `kind`, counting up while more land under the same facet, and dismissed by clicking it (delegates straight to the existing `handleFacetSelect` facet-jump) or by any other facet change. `VirtualList` (`timeline/VirtualList.tsx`) separately gained generic tail-follow: an `atTailRef` recorded only on real scroll events (never recomputed merely because an append changed `totalHeight`) gates whether an `items` growth carries `scrollTop` to the new max — deferring outright to a same-commit `scrollRequest` identity change (a facet-jump or the submit-scroll above) rather than racing it for the same state, and never firing on a shrink (a facet narrowing the stream). This is what a non-user-initiated append (an agent delivery, `TimelinePanel`'s `agent://activity` fold; a future remote collaborator's edit) rides for free without either call site touching `scrollRequest` itself — composing with an active filter structurally, since `VirtualList` only ever sees whatever already-filtered `items` array `TimelinePanel` hands it. PR #504 review round: `atTailRef`'s recording effect took its only reading on `[scrollTop]` alone, so a reader already at the tail at mount — `containerHeight` is still 0 until the measuring `useLayoutEffect` lands, degenerating the tail check to "false for any non-empty stream" — was never followed until their first real scroll; the effect now also re-reads on `containerHeight` (guarded on `!== 0`, so the safe mount default is left alone until a genuine measurement exists, never on `totalHeight` alone so an append still can't retroactively relabel the reader's own choice). Round 2 narrowed that `containerHeight` re-read to the 0 → measured transition only: re-reading on *every* `containerHeight` change relabelled a reader who never moved whenever the container **shrank** (a shrink grows `maxScrollTop` while `scrollTop` stays put and no scroll event fires), silently revoking tail-follow — reachable through this card's own `NewInFacetAffordance`, a `flex: none` sibling that shrinks the list exactly this way when it appears, and through any window shrink on the exported component. `lastReadingRef` now carries the previous `scrollTop`/`containerHeight` so the reading is taken on the first real measurement or a genuine scroll and skipped for a bare resize — while a real scroll landing in the *same* commit as a resize still re-reads, or a reader who scrolled away would stay labelled at-tail and get yanked by the next append (scroll-stealing, the direction Principle 2 actually forbids); both directions are pinned by tests. The prior "a fresh mount ... does carry it" test couldn't discriminate a fix from a no-op (a single 96px row never exceeds the 600px viewport either way); `virtualListTestUtils.ts`'s new `mockClientHeight` patches `HTMLElement.prototype.clientHeight` before `render()` so the first commit already carries a real measurement (a real browser's ordering), letting the two new tests append past the viewport with no scroll event ever fired — of which only the 3-row one actually discriminates (a 0-row mount has `totalHeight === 0`, which makes even the broken reading come out `true`), so that is the load-bearing one to keep. `mockClientHeight`'s restore also now `delete`s the patched getter when there was no own descriptor to put back: jsdom defines `clientHeight` on `Element.prototype`, not `HTMLElement.prototype`, so the original branch never fired and the patch outlived the test on a shared prototype. Round 3: the follow effect itself (not the reading effect rounds 1/2 fixed) had the same stale-state shape one layer over — when a container re-measure and an append land in the *same* commit, `containerHeight` state is still the pre-resize value (the measuring `useLayoutEffect` hasn't flushed), so the target was computed off it and the reader was stranded short of the true new tail, then permanently lost tail-follow since nothing else re-fires the effect to correct it; reachable through an ordinary window resize followed by any append, not a race (no `ResizeObserver`/`resize` listener exists anywhere in `src/`, so a resize is only ever observed on the next commit for some other reason). Fixed by reading `containerRef.current?.clientHeight` directly rather than the `containerHeight` state when computing the follow target, pinned by a new same-commit shrink+append test (the pre-existing "keeps following ... after the container shrinks" test forces the re-measure into its own commit first via an intervening `rerender`, and so cannot discriminate this shape). #1119: a row's height is now a **measurement**, not the caller's constant — `VirtualList` renders each mounted row as an ordinary content-sized `display: flow-root` block (nothing to overflow, so a taller-than-assumed card cannot paint over its neighbour even before any measurement lands; `flow-root` keeps the card's own vertical margins inside the measured box, which collapse straight through a plain wrapper and would leave every spacer short by exactly those margins) and records what it rendered at, by `itemKey`, from a per-commit `getBoundingClientRect` reading plus a per-row `ResizeObserver` for the height changes that come with no re-render at all (a rewrap, a font landing). The scroll container reserves its scrollbar gutter, closing a loop only measured rows can have: an appearing scrollbar narrows every row, narrower rows wrap taller, and a stream near the viewport height would oscillate across that threshold re-measuring each way. A zero reading is never recorded — it means "no layout" (jsdom, a `display: none` ancestor), and recording it would stack the whole stream at offset 0. Measurements outlive a row leaving the window, so scrolling back over seen content never re-derives different offsets; `itemHeightFor`'s constants survive as the **estimate** for a row never yet mounted (`estimatedItemHeight`, renamed for what it is), which is why collapse composition is unchanged. `TimelinePanel.collapse.test.tsx` asserts the collapsed/expanded distinction on the shell each card renders rather than on the row wrapper's inline pixel height, which no longer exists. `virtualization.ts` gained `computeVisibleRangeFromOffsets`: `VirtualList` memoizes the O(items) offsets off the scroll path and both band edges are binary-searched, so a scroll costs O(log items) instead of the scan from index 0 PR #481 documented — without it the 5,000-card scroll benchmark landed at 32.02ms against its 32ms gross-regression line (18.51ms after, against a 21.84ms same-machine baseline). PR #1318 review round 1: measuring rows broke tail-follow, because what a reader at the tail is following is **the content growing**, not the item count growing — and once heights are measured the two come apart. An arriving card has never been mounted, so the commit that grows `items.length` can only lay it out at its *estimate*; its real height lands a commit later, with the count unchanged, so a count-gated follow had already fired for the last time and the reader was left short by the difference, permanently (in Chromium the newest of three arriving cards sat 534px below the fold — further than the viewport is tall). Two things were wrong and both are fixed: the follow now fires on `totalHeight` growth as well as count growth (which also covers a card growing *in place* — a live run gaining an activity line, a rewrap, a font landing — that a count gate could never see at all), and the follow effect records its own pin in `lastReadScrollTopRef` so the `atTailRef` reading does not mistake this component's own write for a reader's scroll: the pin lands on the estimate's tail, the measurement grows the content underneath it, and the re-read would otherwise pair our applied `scrollTop` with the grown total, conclude the reader had scrolled away, and silently revoke tail-follow for the session. Losing items now *revokes* the standing choice rather than skipping a single commit — the replacement stream's own cards are unmeasured too, so a facet switch to fewer but taller cards would otherwise scroll the reader to the tail of a stream they had only just arrived in (caught by this card's own new test before it shipped, not in review); the choice re-establishes on the reader's next real scroll, including the browser's own clamp. jsdom measures every row at 0, so all four tests drive `virtualListTestUtils.ts`'s `mockRowHeights`, and `listOfKeys` gives the facet case a stream whose keys — and therefore whose remembered measurements — genuinely change. #1324 (UXR-Q2, CJ ruling 2026-08-20): submit-scroll now carries `reveal: "full"` on the same `scrollRequest` seam, reducing (`virtualization.ts`'s new `scrollTargetForReveal`) to bottom-anchoring a card that fits the viewport — the whole card visible, extra space above it rather than below the fold — and falling back to the pre-existing top-align once the card is itself taller than the viewport; `reveal` defaults to `"top"`, so P2b-1a's facet-jump is untouched. The card a submit just appended has never been mounted when the jump first applies, so that first pass can only judge "does it fit" off `estimatedItemHeight`, not the card's real rendered height — a message that wraps far past a uniform estimate could otherwise land the bottom-anchored (by-estimate) target and then, once the real (oversized) height measured in, get pinned to the *wrong* edge by the ordinary tail-follow re-pin (which had marked the reader "at the tail" off that same wrong estimate) — the exact "reveals only the top … leaving ~208px below the fold" shape #1324 reported, just with the edges swapped once self-correction ran. `VirtualList` gained a second, `measuredHeights`-keyed effect that corrects the very same jump exactly once more, against now-accurate numbers, the moment the target card's real height lands — declared after the tail-follow "grew" effect, so a same-commit re-measurement can't race the two: effects fire in declaration order, so the "grew" effect's pin (if any) is always the earlier write and the correction effect's own — always freshly recomputed, never derived from what the "grew" effect just wrote — always runs after it. Pinned at the pure-arithmetic level (`virtualization.test.ts`), at the `VirtualList` effect level with the estimate and the real height deliberately equal (no mismatch — isolates "which edge" from "measurement timing") and deliberately mismatched (the actual defect shape, both a too-small and a too-generous estimate), and at the `TimelinePanel` integration level reproducing the issue's own repro (a card whose real height is not known at submit time and measures far past the viewport). PR #1802 review round 1: the correction effect's own "already corrected once" guard (`fullRevealMeasuredRef`) was unpinned — nothing in the suite detected its removal, because a re-run against unchanged offsets recomputes the identical target, a no-op `setScrollTop` regardless of the guard; the new `#1324: does not re-scroll once corrected` test discriminates it by moving the reader away first, so a guard-less re-run would visibly yank them back (mutation-verified: removing the guard turns that test's assertion from `200` to `1920`). A second, separate guard on the tail-follow "grew" effect (`fullRevealPending`, standing it down while a `reveal: "full"` correction was still owed) was equally unpinned and, on inspection, redundant — declaration order already makes the correction effect's write win any same-commit race, and no reachable case lets the "grew" effect's own pin be the *last* write for a pending `reveal: "full"` request; removed rather than pinned (the second of the two options the review offered), taking its rationale paragraph with it. #1727: filtering itself was never broken (that was #1728's duplicate-id defect); the two real defects were a silent blank panel and two orphan facets. `Facet` gained an `emptyLabel` (`facets.ts`, exported `facetById` for the lookup) and `TimelinePanel` renders it in place of `VirtualList` whenever `filteredCards` is empty, matching `ProjectRail`'s "No files yet" precedent (Principle 7) rather than a copy-pasted generic string. `document-birth` and `project-grounding` remain deliberately unclaimed by any kind-scoped facet — which facet (if any) should claim them is a J#/Designer call the issue raises, not an Engineer's guess — so `facets.test.ts` gained an exhaustive `Record<TimelineCard["kind"], …>` table over all six kinds (not the three `mixedFacetCards` cycled) that pins today's two orphans and fails to compile if a seventh kind is ever added without a matching table entry. |
| NI-timeline-tail-follow | a reader sitting at the tail is carried to each arriving card in **real layout** — the measured-height half of tail-follow (UXR-Q2 / P2b-1c, ux-design §4 "Scroll motion") | e2e | a reader sitting at the tail is carried to each arriving card that renders taller than the height assumed for it | present | #1119 (PR #1318 review round 1): the sibling `NI-timeline-correctness` vitest tests reach this through `mockRowHeights`, but jsdom measures every row at 0, so the production shape — a card laid out at its assumed height and *measured* a commit later — only exists in a real browser. The scenario deliberately scrolls away and back to the tail, then lands three tall document-birth cards one at a time and requires every arrival to settle within 2px of the measured tail. #1826 CI exposed a second real-layout edge: after the arrival was measured and followed, the outer layout could settle the rail 38px shorter without another React commit. `VirtualList` now observes its container and re-pins only while the reader's standing choice is still tail; the direct vitest resize regression mutates this branch deterministically. Non-vacuous: the stream must overflow its rail by >200px and the newest card's top must remain above the fold. |
| NI-a11y-severity | severity color + letter rule (not color alone) | vitest | a severity-tagged thread renders a severity chip carrying both colour and letter, a thread with no severity renders no severity chip, an empty-string severity still renders a chip with a non-blank letter, an Object.prototype-keyed severity renders its fallback letter rather than crashing the card, maps the long-form spellings to their ux-design.md §2 letter, is case-insensitive, passes an already-single-letter severity through uppercased, falls back to the first letter uppercased for an unrecognized severity, falls back to a non-empty placeholder letter for an empty severity, falls back to a non-empty placeholder letter for a whitespace-only severity, maps the same ratified spellings severityLetter does case-insensitively, returns undefined for an unranked severity that merely starts with a ratified letter, returns undefined for an empty or whitespace-only severity, returns undefined for an Object.prototype key rather than an inherited builtin, takes the first-letter fallback for an Object.prototype key instead of an inherited builtin, renders both the colour hook and the letter — never colour alone, maps every J3 severity level to its display letter, renders no mark at all for a thread with no severity, is keyed to the exact same anchor the highlight uses, gives an orphaned severity-tagged thread no marker rather than a wrong one (D17/P2), renders its gutter column even with no severity-tagged threads | present | P2b-5b (#355) landed the timeline-dot half: `comments/CommentThreadCard`'s severity chip (`● {letter}`, the letter as plain text content so it reads without colour, mirroring `timeline/ChangeSetCard`'s identical chip) and `comments/severity.ts`'s free-form-string → letter mapping. P2b-4b (#353) lands the editor margin-mark half: `editor/severityMarginMarks.ts`'s CM6 gutter renders `● {letter}` beside a severity-tagged thread's anchored line, built from `editor/commentHighlights.ts`'s `commentAnchors`/`severityOf` (the exact live-anchor field the sibling highlight decoration draws from, so a mark can never point at a different line than its highlight), and skips ordinary human-comment threads (D6) and orphaned/document-level ones (D17/P2) for the identical reason `commentAnchors` already excludes them. Both halves' colour rule is applied by keying the same `data-severity` letter off the same `--color-danger-fg`/`--color-warning-fg`/`--color-muted` tokens (J3's ratified red/amber/gray) — but jsdom applies no stylesheet, so no test in this repo executes the colour itself; every vitest assertion above covers the letter/attribute/position half only (review-gate finding on PR #443, carried forward for the margin-mark half by the same limitation). The margin gutter's *column* is unconditional — installed with the extension, so a findings-free document still reserves its 2.2em width; accepted deliberately (a column that appeared on the first finding would shift text sideways unasked, against UX Principle 2) and pinned by `renders its gutter column even with no severity-tagged threads`, since the width itself is unexecutable CSS. Whether 2.2em is the right idle cost, and whether the column should be scoped to review-mode documents, is left to the wiring card (#259) — review-gate finding on PR #476. `severity.ts`'s ratified-spelling table is a `Map`, not an object literal, because the key is an unvalidated agent-supplied string (`store::comments::Thread.severity` is a free-form `Option<String>` by ratified design): an object lookup inherits `Object.prototype`, so `"__proto__"`/`"constructor"` answered with a builtin instead of a letter — a non-string chip value that throws in `CommentThreadCard`'s render and degrades `severityMarginMarks` to `● [object Object]`. Pinned at both call sites by the two `Object.prototype key` cases above (review finding on PR #613, #537). |

| NI-cm6-position-space | **CM6 position space vs serialized-string offsets** (#447, 2026-07-21) — the second coordinate axis, independent of the byte↔UTF-16 one `textops::Utf16Map` already handles: CM6 counts every line break as exactly **one** position whatever its literal bytes, while `serializeDocumentState` rejoins lines with the document's real `lineBreak` (two UTF-16 units on CRLF). This row closes the **disk→CM6** counterpart and gives the axis one named home. `EditorShell` was calling `parseChanges(displayContent)`/`parseChanges(diskContent)` — the file's own CRLF text — and feeding the returned UTF-16 offsets straight to CM6 as decoration positions *and* as the ranges `decisionsTransaction` replaces on accept/reject. So on a CRLF document every redline sat one-unit-per-preceding-line-break too far right, and an accept/reject **edited the wrong range of the file**: not cosmetic, a silent corruption of the source of truth (TD-2, P1 — the issue's own "severity may be higher than it looks", confirmed by the accept-path test above, which mangles the document on the pre-fix code). Fixed by `document.ts`'s new `editorOffsetText(state)` (`Text.toString()`, exactly the string CM6 positions index by construction — a *coordinate projection*, never a normalization: it is not what goes to disk, `serializeDocumentState` still is) used at all three offset-paired seams: the mount-time redline seed, the external-change Reload re-seed, and `handleQuote`'s anchor capture (whose inline `doc.toString()` from #446 folds into the shared helper, so the reasoning lives in one place rather than being restated per call site — the "shared helper" the issue asked for). The `parseChanges`/`captureAnchor` docs in `editor/ipc.ts` now state the requirement at the boundary itself. The shell tests drive a CriticMarkup-aware stand-in that derives its offsets from whatever content it is handed, exactly as the backend does — a fixed-offset mock would pass on the buggy code. **#1326/#1799:** `document.ts`'s `projectDiskRangeToBufferPositions` is the disk→CM6 adapter for backend-resolved comment anchors. It accepts either exact coordinate form the current buffer can prove: LF text already identical to `editorOffsetText(state)` (including a normalizing save), or raw text identical to `serializeDocumentState(state)` (including CRLF load and the FR-ED-2 opt-out). Raw ranges are projected through the document's real line map, subtracting the extra UTF-16 units occupied by multi-unit separators from both boundaries; malformed boundaries inside a separator, stale/session-restored disk text, and normalization outputs that do not describe the buffer refuse instead of clamping or guessing (D17/FR-CMT-6, P2). See the FR-CMT-1 row below for the three `EditorShell` seed paths using the shared adapter. |

helper), `store::docs_map` (pure map + I/O), `store::service::SidecarStore` (append/fold-from-disk for both sidecars), `store::watch::SidecarWatcher` (`notify`'s recommended — native, non-polling — watcher, classifying raw paths into typed per-doc changes). Doc-identity lifecycle tests (mint/rename/adopt/delete) are named under FR-ED-8 above rather than repeated here. **#615 (flake fix):** the two live-watch tests asserted on whatever `recv_timeout` handed back *first*; on macOS, FSEvents can legitimately deliver a spurious event for the freshly-watched root directory before the one a test wants, so a leading `Other`-classified event flipped the assertion (test-plan §7: an intermittent failure counts as a failure). Fixed by draining to the matching event instead of trusting event #1: a new `store::watch::recv_matching` (test-only, predicate-based) generalizes the existing `store::service`-only `recv_for_doc` drain pattern, now shared across both call sites (Rule of Three) plus `a_write_under_the_watched_directory_is_reported`'s new one; `no_event_within_the_timeout_is_a_defined_recv_timeout_error` now drains-and-ignores any such self-event on its way to asserting the eventual timeout. `recv_matching_drains_past_a_leading_event_that_does_not_match` exercises the drain deterministically against a synthetic channel (no dependence on real OS event timing) to prove the mechanism rather than hoping a real watcher reproduces the flake on demand. |

No migration is provided (TD-29), so these tests pin that Emend neither reads, writes nor deletes `.cowrite/`. |
| NI-project-lock | Per-project ownership registry (test-plan §3 "Stores"; TD §3.5 finding M7; TD-20 rulings (2)/(3); TD-12 amended 2026-08-14): taking ownership of a project writes every artifact — the content-free OS `lock`, the unlocked `owner.json`, the unlocked `lock.focus-request` — under `<app_local_data_dir>/instances/<blake3(canonical root)>/` and **nothing** under the project root, so an ordinary project open leaves only sanctioned (TD-4 portable) files in `.emend/`; `owner.json` carries a `schema_version` (it is a cross-process record two builds may share) and records holder instance id, pid, executable identity, acquired-at **and the canonical project root**, so a reader holding only the hashed registry directory can say which project it belongs to without re-hashing candidate roots; every spelling of one root — relative respelling, trailing separator, differing case on Windows/macOS, and a directory link (a junction on Windows, a symlink on Unix) — resolves to one registry directory while genuinely distinct roots never collide; a second instance opening an already-held project does not acquire it and instead signals the owning instance to focus its window; the TD-17 `OpenProject` aggregate owns the guard, so all three session ends — replace, explicit close, owning-process exit — release ownership through the same drop that unbinds the MCP server, and the *ordering* that guarantees it (the claim declared last, so dropped last) is pinned by reading the declaration — a source-text assertion, so it pins declaration order and not teardown order, and it is blind to a hand-written `Drop`, to a field its line-based parser cannot see, and to *why* any given field must precede the claim; a registry left behind by a crashed instance is reclaimed rather than permanently blocking; and `store::gitignore::ensure_pattern` retains exactly one production caller (`store::tab_session`), with the old `lock*` pattern surviving in no shipped Rust source | rust
