ensure_pattern_has_exactly_one_production_caller, no_shipped_source_still_writes_the_relocated_registrys_gitignore_pattern | present | P2a-11 (#152); relocated by P2a-11b (#1746). `store::lock` keeps its whole design — a content-free OS file lock (`fs4`, no libc) whose scope is the open handle rather than a PID check, so the OS releases it the instant that handle closes (crash included) and stale reclaim needs no liveness step, plus **unlocked** sidecars because Windows `LockFileEx` is mandatory and per-handle (TD-12's finding, untouched) — and changes only the directory it is handed. That directory is now per-user machine-local app data (`registry_dir(app_local_data_dir, root)`, keyed by `store::project_identity::project_key`'s `blake3(canonical root)` — the one rule D15/P2a-17 and TD-20 ruling (3) share, extracted to a single home by #2231), never `<root>/.emend/`: a lock inside the project folder is visible to every user and machine sharing it, and `acquire` used to write into the user's repo on every open. `lock.owner` became `owner.json` and carries the canonical project root, so a `blake3`-named directory is identifiable for support/debugging, plus the executable identity TD-20 ruling (6) needs to defeat pid reuse before any activation grant. `OpenProject` holds the `ProjectLockGuard` for the session with **no accessor and no take path**, declared last in the struct so field-order drop releases ownership only *after* the server unbinds — the falsifier being an ownership release that leaves the MCP server bound. **Nothing migrated:** `store::lock` had no production caller, so no shipped path ever wrote `.cowrite/lock*`; an inert `lock*` line in a `.cowrite/.gitignore` from a pre-TD-20 development build is left alone (TD-12 amended). **Not claimed here:** acquiring ownership still has no production caller — `instances/` acquires and hands over the guard at P2a-11c..g, so today the guard reaches `OpenProject::open_owning` only from tests; and the genuinely cross-process *concurrency* case (two processes racing one root) remains P2a-11d's real-OS rendezvous test. `losing_the_owning_process_releases_the_projects_ownership` spawns a second process solely because release-on-exit is unassertable inside one, and races nothing. The three gitignore-named tests this row previously carried (`acquiring_ensures_runtime_lock_files_are_gitignored`, `an_existing_cowrite_gitignore_keeps_its_entries_when_the_exclusion_is_added`, `a_gitignore_that_already_has_the_exclusion_is_left_byte_identical`) described behavior TD-12's amendment removes; they are replaced — not silently dropped — by the positive `.emend/` allowlist check and the two `store::gitignore` caller-set checks, and `ensure_pattern`'s own behavior stays covered by its module tests under `FR-ED-3`. |
| NI-project-key | **One project-identity rule** (TD-20 ruling (3); D15/P2a-17): `canonical_root`, `project_key` and the `<app_local_data_dir>/<area>/<key>/` layout have exactly one implementation (`store::project_identity`), and every per-project app-local-data store — the D15 transcript log, the adoption log, TD-20's ownership registry, and the FR-ED-3 tab-session writer lease — resolves the **same** per-project directory `<area>/<key>/` through `project_dir` for the same root, asserted by reading the path each store itself resolves rather than re-deriving a key, and no shipped module can compose an app-local-data per-project path of its own, because `project_key` is **private** to that module and `project_dir` is the only reachable way to a per-project directory (#2245); the load-bearing input is a root whose `to_string_lossy()` and `as_encoded_bytes()` forms differ, since on an ordinary root every spelling agrees and the assertion would be vacuous; the key hashes the canonical root's **UTF-8 bytes**, byte-identical to both pre-#2231 spellings for every valid-Unicode root (so no on-disk store moves) and pinned by a golden digest a toolchain upgrade cannot shift, while a root that is not valid Unicode hashes a tagged, lossless, per-platform-documented encoding so two genuinely distinct roots can never collide; the fallible disposition (`require_canonical_root` — TD-20 ruling (4)'s refuse-before-touching-the-registry rule) and the raw-path fallback share the one canonicalization call; and no shipped backend source hashes its own path bytes or redefines the canonical-root rule | rust | every_per_project_store_lays_its_key_out_as_one_area_then_the_key, every_per_project_store_keys_one_root_the_same_way, an_ordinary_unicode_root_also_keys_one_directory_in_every_store, two_roots_that_differ_only_outside_unicode_never_share_a_key, a_valid_unicode_root_keys_exactly_where_it_already_did, a_projects_key_is_a_fixed_value_a_toolchain_upgrade_cannot_move, the_same_project_root_always_yields_the_same_key, distinct_project_roots_yield_distinct_keys, a_root_that_does_not_exist_still_yields_a_deterministic_key_not_a_panic, a_root_that_cannot_be_canonicalized_is_a_refusal_for_a_caller_that_needs_one, project_dir_is_the_area_then_the_key, only_sanctioned_sources_hash_their_own_bytes, only_sanctioned_test_modules_re_derive_the_key, the_canonical_root_rule_has_exactly_one_implementation, no_shipped_source_still_hashes_a_paths_lossy_or_encoded_bytes | present | #2231. Before this row, "blake3(canonical root)" was **two** implementations hashing different bytes: `store::transcript::project_key` hashed `to_string_lossy().as_bytes()` (transcript, adoption, and — since #1746 — the ownership registry), while `commands::tabs` hashed `as_os_str().as_encoded_bytes()` for its writer lease; the canonicalization rule itself had two spellings, `store::transcript::canonical_root` (raw-path fallback) and a fallible one private to `commands::tabs`. The two byte spellings agree for every valid-Unicode path and diverge once a path is not, so the defect was latent — which is why the divergence test picks the non-Unicode root and why per-store key assertions would have proved nothing. **Encoding choice (#2231):** neither pre-existing spelling is fit for a key stored on disk. `to_string_lossy()` is not injective — every non-Unicode unit becomes `U+FFFD`, so two distinct roots key one store, and silently sharing one user's transcript log between two projects is the worst failure available (D15). `as_encoded_bytes()` is injective but `std` documents its encoding as unspecified and "only comparable within the same Rust version built for the same target platform", saying outright that storing it in a file "will likely result in incompatible byte slices" — the identical objection `project_key` already records for rejecting `std`'s `DefaultHasher`. So the encoding is stated by us: UTF-8 bytes for a Unicode root (what both old spellings produced, hence no migration), and a NUL-tagged `encode_wide` (Windows, big-endian) / raw-bytes (Unix) escape otherwise. **Nothing on disk moved:** the two behavioural store tests cover the Unicode and non-Unicode roots, and `a_valid_unicode_root_keys_exactly_where_it_already_did` re-derives both pre-#2231 spellings outside the shared rule and asserts the new key equals each. The only paths whose key changes are roots that are not valid Unicode — unreachable on APFS/HFS+, and never minted by Win32 — where the old transcript rule was already conflating distinct projects, so there is no coherent key to preserve; a tab-session lease is in any case a transient advisory lock file recreated on demand, not durable data, and cannot outlive the restart an upgrade requires. **#2245 (layout):** #2231 unified the key but left the lease the one store composing its own path — `tab-session-writers/<key>.lock`, a file named by the key where the other three name a directory — so `project_dir` was the layout authority for three of the four. The lease now resolves `tab-session-writers/<key>/lock` through `project_dir` (leaf spelled `lock`, as `store::lock`'s sibling OS lock already is), and the behavioural tests read each store's per-project *directory* rather than the lease's file stem, so one rule pins one shape and not merely one key; **Enforcement is visibility, not a scan (#2245 review).** The first draft of the layout guard scanned for the string `project_identity::project_key` while claiming "an app-local-data per-project path must be composed by `project_dir`" — a claim it did not enforce: the reviewer made `store::adoption` compose its own path through `use super::project_identity::*;`, which contains no such string, and the whole lib suite passed 699/0. So does a module alias, and so does a braced import list (`use crate::store::project_identity::{project_key};`) — the very idiom `store::lock` already uses for `project_dir`; a renamed import through the full path is the one escape that needle did catch. `project_key` is therefore **private**, with a `#[cfg(test)] project_key_for_layout_tests` accessor for the two sibling stores' own layout tests; the same mutation now fails to compile (`E0425: cannot find function project_key in this scope` … "exists but is inaccessible", with the compiler suggesting `project_dir`). Production reach is the compiler's to refuse; `only_sanctioned_test_modules_re_derive_the_key` asserts only what is left for a scan — which `#[cfg(test)]` modules re-derive the key — and says so. **Moving the path costs nothing:** the lease file is content-free — never read, never written, its only state the OS lock held on the open handle and released by the OS on process exit including a crash — so it is advisory within a session and not load-bearing across restarts; no build has shipped (the repo has no release and only the `v1-legacy` tag) and the real app-local-data directory on the only machine holds `EBWebView/` and `transcripts/` with no `tab-session-writers/` at all, so nothing is orphaned. A flat `<key>.lock` left by a development build is a zero-byte file and is left alone rather than swept, the disposition TD-12's amendment already records for an inert pre-relocation artifact. The one residual is a *concurrently running* pair of builds straddling the change, which would lock two different paths and both believe they hold the writer lane; that needs two builds of the app running at once, which is a development-only situation and cannot arise for a user of an unshipped app. |
| NI-local-transcript-store | Local transcript store (D15, TD §3.1 "store/ owns... local transcript DB", TD-5): append-only per-project JSONL log of rendered `TimelineCard` events under the OS app-local-data dir (never `.emend/`, never repo-synced, never provider session state); append→read-back is order-preserving; a missing file rehydrates to empty rather than erroring; a crash-truncated trailing line is skipped, not fatal; already-appended events survive a simulated process restart; concurrent in-process appends never interleave or corrupt lines (single-writer-per-project safety, consistent with P2a-11's `store::lock` discipline) | rust | a_chat_message_event_round_trips_through_json_fields_and_all, kind_specific_fields_are_flattened_onto_the_same_json_object, an_event_with_a_future_kinds_unknown_fields_still_round_trips, transcript_file_is_laid_out_under_transcripts_slash_the_project_key, reading_a_missing_file_is_an_empty_history_not_an_error, append_then_read_back_preserves_order, append_creates_missing_parent_directories, src-tauri/src/store/transcript.rs::each_append_is_a_new_line_never_a_rewrite_of_earlier_lines , a_process_restart_does_not_lose_already_appended_events, a_corrupt_trailing_line_is_skipped_rather_than_failing_the_whole_read, concurrent_appends_from_multiple_threads_never_corrupt_or_interleave_lines | present | P2a-17 (#161): `store::transcript` — a schema-versioned (`schema_version`) envelope (`id`/`kind`/`timestamp`, mirroring `TimelineCardBase`) with kind-specific fields flattened through opaque, so a new frontend `TimelineCard` variant needs no Rust change. One transcript file per project under `<app_local_data_dir>/transcripts/<key>/transcript.jsonl`, keyed through the one project-identity rule every app-local-data store shares (`store::project_identity`, row `NI-project-key`) — this module no longer implements the key, and the three generic key-rule tests moved there with it (#2231). `append` holds an `fs4` exclusive lock for the duration of each write — belt-and-suspenders on top of, not a replacement for, P2a-11's per-project OS lock. `commands::transcript::{append_transcript_event,load_transcript}` resolve the real app-local-data dir from the Tauri app handle (thin IPC, no direct commands-layer tests, matching `commands::settings`'s precedent). |
| NI-local-transcript-persistence | `timeline/` wiring of the local transcript store (P2a-17, D15): `TimelinePanel` rehydrates its prior history from the store on mount when given a project `root`, prepending it to any already-seeded cards; every newly submitted card is also persisted; without a `root` the panel stays purely in-memory, matching P2a-16's original behavior, with no backend calls at all; a rehydrate or persist failure degrades gracefully — never a crash, never an unhandled rejection, never rolling back the optimistic in-memory append. **#1727 (ux-design §2, review round 2):** the panel distinguishes an in-flight load from a successful empty result, so facet-empty copy never fires before history answers. **#1813 (ratified by CJ 2026-08-26):** rejection is a third state: preserve in-memory cards, show `Activity unavailable` / `Earlier activity could not be loaded.` / `[Try again]`, retry transcript rehydration only, and never show completed-empty copy for the failed load. **#1395:** outside a Tauri webview (D23) the bridge reaches the headless test driver over `callBackend` instead of degrading to a no-op/empty result on its own — a `callBackend` rejection propagates uncaught from the bridge itself, and every production caller (`TimelinePanel`'s rehydrate effect and persist call) decides its own degrade | vitest | appendTranscriptEvent invokes returned them (append order), appendTranscriptEvent propagates a callBackend rejection, loadTranscript propagates a callBackend rejection, rehydrates its prior history from the transcript store on card to the transcript store when a root is given, a persist failure does not crash the panel or roll back the optimistic append, does not show the empty-facet message while the initial transcript load for this root is still in flight, does not flash a false empty-facet message over a project's actual history on first paint, shows the ratified unavailable state instead of a false empty claim when transcript rehydrate fails, retries transcript rehydration only and restores history after a successful retry, keeps in-memory activity visible alongside the unavailable state after rehydrate fails | present | P2a-17 (#161): `timeline/ipc.ts` mirrors `store::transcript::TranscriptEvent` (cross-boundary types defined in Rust, mirrored in TS per CLAUDE.md). `TimelinePanel`'s optional `root` prop wires this in: a `useEffect` keyed on `root` loads history once per mount, and `handleSubmit` best-effort-persists (`void appendTranscriptEvent(...).catch(() => {})`) alongside the existing in-memory append; both the rehydrate and the persist call handle rejection (loopback review finding on PR #220) so store I/O failure never surfaces as an unhandled rejection or a lost message. **#1727/#1813:** `transcriptLoadState` keeps `loading`, `loaded`, and `failed` distinct. Only `loaded` plus an empty filtered stream renders the facet-empty `<p>`; `failed` renders the established compact unavailable treatment as a sibling of the stream, so retained cards remain visible. Its button moves back to `loading` and increments a transcript-only attempt dependency; a successful retry prepends returned history and returns to `loaded`, while no other activity or persistence concern restarts. **#1395 (test-plan §7b Corollary B):** `appendTranscriptEvent`/`loadTranscript` route through `callBackend`, which reaches `crates/test-driver`'s `/append_transcript_event`/`/load_transcript` routes (backed by the same `store::transcript::append`/`read_all` the Tauri command calls) outside a Tauri webview; `TimelinePanel` owns the visible rejection degrade. |

Removing either `appendTranscriptEvent`, `loadTranscript`, or startup hydration from the production panel/app makes this journey fail while its in-session pre-reload assertion still passes. |
| NI-timeline-card-id-uniqueness | A timeline card id is unique for the lifetime of the transcript it is persisted into (D15), not merely within one app session — the identity a rehydrated history is keyed by. The generator is `crypto.randomUUID`-backed wherever the webview exposes it (the same restart-unique shape the backend's own `run-<uuid>` uses) and salted per process where it does not\, so a fresh module registry — what a relaunched app hands the next session — can never repeat an id an earlier session already persisted. Ids already on disk from the pre-#1728 counter are repaired **as they load**: each repeat after the first takes a derived `<id>#n` that skips any spelling the history itself persisted\, first occurrences and unrepeated ids pass through byte-identical\, and nothing is written back (the transcript is append-only D15 scaffolding\, not a `.emend/` portability contract) | vitest | never repeats an id a previous session already persisted, src/timeline/types.test.ts::keeps that guarantee where the webview exposes no crypto.randomUUID , mints uuid-shaped ids on the production path\, not the sequential counter, rekeys a repeated id so a rehydrated history has no duplicate keys, leaves a history whose ids are merely similar exactly as persisted, never spends an id another event in the same history persisted under its own name, rehydrates a pre-#1728 history whose events share one id as separate\, distinctly keyed rows | present | #1728 (root cause of #1718/#1727): `timeline/types.ts`'s `nextId` was a module-scoped counter that reset with the process\, so persisted ids repeated across launches — CJ's own store holds eight `project-grounding-1` events. Duplicate ids are duplicate `VirtualList` React keys\, which is why the stream stopped reconciling on a facet change. Every test here crosses a module-registry or persistence boundary (`vi.resetModules()`\, a rehydrated history): minting two ids inside one process and asserting they differ passes with the colliding counter too. The read-time repair follows `tolerateLegacyChangeIds`' precedent in the same module. The Tauri webview's own `crypto.randomUUID` exposure is not executed by any test in this repo (no suite runs in WKWebView/WebView2\, and the D23 e2e lane runs in a browser) — the salted fallback is what makes that unexecuted branch safe rather than assumed. `mintRunId`'s identical counter defect — since #1760 only the no-backend fallback\, but still stamped into the published comment-event sidecar — is #1782\, filed not fixed. |
| NI-reveal-atomic-traversal | Reveal-at-cursor is an app-global, durable, machine-local preference that defaults OFF (#2389/TD-26). When ON it reveals structural Markdown and CriticMarkup delimiters only on the active line in Rich text + Display All. Markdown and other display projections suppress the effect without rewriting the saved preference; returning to All restores it. Hidden markers remain atomic and every preference/mode/cursor transition is presentation-only. | vitest | defaults to hidden markup even on the active line (#2389), reveals (does not hide) markup on the active line, hides markup once the cursor leaves the line, contains exactly the hidden-marker ranges — never emphasis/strong spans or blockquote lines, excludes markup that reveal-at-cursor has made visible on the active line, markup stays hidden on what would otherwise be the active line once reveal is suppressed, re-enabling reveal restores the active-line reveal, toggling reveal-suppression rebuilds the decoration set with no doc or line change, never rewrites the stored reveal preference while changing projections (#2389), updates decorations when only the reveal preference changes (#2389), arrowing right across a hidden heading marker jumps over it as a single unit, arrowing left back across the same hidden marker jumps over it as a single unit, arrowing across a hidden list marker is likewise atomic, without atomicRanges wired the same movement would land inside the hidden marker (baseline), moving within an active (revealed) line is ordinary single-character motion not atomic, a traversal sequence across multiple hidden markers never mutates the document | present | P2a-4 (#154), #216, and #2389/TD-26. `revealAtCursorEnabledField` stores only the preference (default false); structural and redline builders combine it with `displayModeAllowsMarkupReveal`. Display controls dispatch only their own projection effect. `DocumentSurface` seeds and later updates the preference in place. The original SPIKE-3 manual feel caveat for CSS easing remains; automated tests pin traversal and zero document mutation. |
| NI-viewport-scoped-decorations | Structural decoration build cost is bounded by what's on screen, not by document size (TD-13/SPIKE-3 follow-up, #216, deferred Important finding from #153's review): the live decoration layer walks only `view.visibleRanges`, rebuilding on a doc edit, a viewport move (scroll/resize), an active-line change, the reveal-suppression/view-mode effects, or the syntax tree itself completing a parse (#391) — never on an unrelated same-viewport, same-line selection nudge; a construct outside the viewport at mount time is not decorated until scrolled into view, and scrolling it into view decorates it without ever mutating the document | vitest | a same-line selection change does not rebuild the decoration set, a cross-line caret move rebuilds the decoration set and reveals the newly-active line, toggling reveal-suppression rebuilds the decoration set with no doc or line change, switching source view rebuilds the decoration set with no doc or line change, scopes the build to the visible viewport: content far below the initial viewport is not yet decorated, scrolling the far heading into view rebuilds and decorates it, still decorates the far heading when mount's initial parse budget would otherwise expire (#478), never mutates the document across a viewport-driven rebuild, rebuilds once a starved initial parse's tree completes with no intervening edit/scroll/caret move (#391) | present | #216: `editor/structuralDecorations.ts`'s `structuralDecorationsField` (a `StateField`, #153) is replaced by `structuralDecorationsPlugin` (a `ViewPlugin`) so the build can read `view.visibleRanges` — `buildStructural` takes an optional offset-range scope (defaulting to the whole doc for the plain-`EditorState` correctness suites above, which stay exactly as they were). Only testable via a mounted `EditorView`, since a bare `EditorState` has no viewport; the fixture is kept small so a mount-time parse is cheap, but small is *not* by itself a guarantee of a complete tree: `Language.state`'s initial parse is bounded by a 20 ms **wall-clock** budget (`Work.Apply`) as well as the 3000-char initial viewport, so a contended machine can truncate even a tiny fixture. **#478 (flake root-cause, sibling of #368/#391 for this mounted-`ViewPlugin` block):** "scrolling the far heading into view rebuilds and decorates it" failed intermittently under full-suite parallel load — not a viewport-recompute timing race as first suspected (`ViewState.update`'s viewport recompute is synchronous within `dispatch`), but the same initial-parse-budget race `NI-parse-budget-determinism` (#368) closed for the plain-`EditorState` suites above, reached here via the mounted-view path instead: under contention the far heading's node was never parsed, so the viewport-scoped rebuild (which does fire) walked an incomplete tree and found nothing to decorate. Reproduced deterministically by injecting the same clock-driven starvation #368's regression tests use, then fixed at the root by making this block's own `mount` helper call `forceParsing` (`@codemirror/language`) before returning — the same idiom `tests/perf/structuralDecorationsViewport.perf.test.ts`'s `mount` already uses (PR #287) — rather than adding a condition-based wait around the assertion, since the actual race is parse completeness, not viewport timing. **#391 (fixed here):** #478 papered over the mounted plugin's blindness to parse progress by forcing every test's own mount to a *complete* parse before asserting anything — it never exercised what happens when a genuinely truncated parse completes with no other trigger, which is exactly production's shape (CM6's `ParseWorker` publishes the finished tree via a bare `Language.setState` effect — no doc change, no viewport move, no active-line move, none of this module's own effects). `structuralInputsChanged` now also rebuilds when `syntaxTree(update.startState) !== syntaxTree(update.state)`. The regression test mounts *without* forcing the parse, under the same `withStarvedParseClock` starvation #368/#478 use, on a fixture (`fixtureWithFarHeading({ midHeadingLine })`) probed to leave a heading inside the initial viewport genuinely unparsed after one starved parse step (a lone/early heading finishes regardless of the clock — empirically verified against `syntaxTree(view.state).length` directly — so the fixture has to place the target heading past the long single-paragraph run that follows the first one); it then completes the parse via `forceParsing` (no scroll/edit/caret move) and asserts the decoration appears. A dedicated perf benchmark (test-plan §6, not a PR/CI gate) lives at `tests/perf/structuralDecorationsViewport.perf.test.ts` (`npm run test:perf`), asserting keystroke-path latency on a SPIKE-3-basis ~1MB/500-region document stays within the FR-X-4 budget and, on a document scaled to ~20,000 regions (forcing a full synchronous parse first via `forceParsing` — PR #287 review finding, see the file's docblocks — so the benchmark measures a real full-size tree rather than Lezer's few-KB synchronous-parse budget), does not scale with region count; verified to fail as expected against #153's pre-migration `StateField` in a detached worktree before being accepted. The `syntaxTree` gate added for #391 does not change these numbers (re-measured post-fix: keystroke p95 1.27ms, small/large-doc keystroke medians 0.64ms/5.11ms) because a doc edit already short-circuits `structuralInputsChanged` before the new check is reached. |
| NI-comment-highlight-viewport-scope | The comment-anchor highlight layer (§3.3's second decoration family) obeys the same two invariants `NI-viewport-scoped-decorations` pins for structural markup: build cost is bounded by what's on screen (an anchor far below the viewport is not decorated until scrolled into view), and the layer is read-only — no seed, edit, or viewport move it reacts to ever mutates the document (P1) | vitest | decorates an anchor inside the viewport, does not build a decoration for an anchor far below the viewport, decorates the far anchor once it is scrolled into view, never mutates the document across a rebuild (P1), never mutates the document on a click (P1) | present | P2b-4a (#352): `editor/commentHighlights.ts`'s `commentHighlightsPlugin` — a `ViewPlugin` for exactly #216's reason (only a plugin can read `view.visibleRanges`), so a document carrying hundreds of threads pays only for the visible ones on the keystroke path (FR-X-4). The split differs from the structural family's: anchor *positions* live in a whole-document `StateField` (`commentAnchorsField`) because an anchor's position is a property of the document, not of what's on screen — it must stay correct offscreen so scrolling one into view draws it in the right place — while only the `Decoration.mark` construction is viewport-scoped. The scoping assertion was mutation-checked: dropping the `visibleRanges` filter makes "does not build a decoration for an anchor far below the viewport" fail, so the row is not self-satisfying. No perf benchmark of its own — the mark-construction path this scopes is O(visible threads) with no tree walk, unlike the structural family's. |

`editor/selectionWatcher.ts` (new) — a CM6 update-listener extension reporting the live non-empty selection (offset/length/text, CodeMirror's own UTF-16 space), deduped against its own last-reported value; purely observational (no `dispatch` of its own), which is what makes "never steals the caret or scrolls" (Principle 2) true by construction rather than by a special-cased check. `editor/QuoteAffordance.tsx` (new) is the presentational "Quote this in a message" button, positioned by `EditorShell` via `EditorView.coordsAtPos` (mirrors `RedlineReview`'s identical popover-positioning helper — a second, small occurrence left un-extracted per CLAUDE.md's Rule of Three) and shown only once a caller actually supplies `onQuoteSelection` (no dead button before an owner exists to receive it, e.g. before #409 mounts the timeline). Clicking it calls the new `commands::document::capture_anchor` Tauri command (rust row below) against the *live* buffer text (mirrors `parse_changes`'s content-not-path shape, so a dirty, unsaved selection still anchors correctly) via `editor/ipc.ts`'s `captureAnchor`, then hands `{text, anchor}` up — never touching the CM6 view itself, so a capture failure (`onError`) or success alike leaves focus and scroll exactly where they were. **Review fix (PR #446, invariant 4 — D17, TD-6, P2, FR-CMT-6):** `handleQuote` hands `capture_anchor` `state.doc.toString()` (always LF-joined, CM6's own coordinate space), not `serializeDocumentState` (CRLF-joined on a CRLF document) — pairing the CM6 selection offset with the latter drifted by one UTF-16 unit per preceding line break, a well-formed but silently wrong anchor on every CRLF document. Pinned by the new CRLF-mounted vitest case above and `capture_anchor_indexes_crlf_content_in_its_own_utf16_space` in the rust row below. `timeline/MessageComposer.tsx` (new) is the composer's quote state: attaches a distinct incoming quote (tracked by object identity — every real quote click mints a fresh object), is dismissible via `timeline/QuoteBar.tsx` (new) without sending, travels into `onSubmit`'s second argument, and clears the same way a dismiss does once sent — state is adjusted during render (not a `useEffect`) so a genuinely new incoming quote lands in the same commit rather than flashing the old one for a frame. `timeline/types.ts` gains `QuotedExcerpt`/`ChatMessageCard.quote` (mirrors `Anchor` from `comments/types.ts`, the TD-4 shared shape) and `createChatMessageCard`'s options; `TimelinePanel` wires `MessageComposer` in place of the bare `MessageInput` and threads a `quote` into the card it creates. `timeline/ChatMessageCard.tsx` renders the quoted excerpt as a `blockquote` with a clickable deep-link (`onFocusQuote`, mirrors `ChangeSetCard`'s identical `onFocusChange` convention: called only on an explicit click, never automatically, and omitted-safe with no live editor to scroll yet). **Scope boundary honored (per the issue):** the v1 baseline's auto-created linked comment from a quoted selection is deliberately not built here — D13 does not carry it forward and no FR-CMT requirement names it; this card is selection → quote bar → anchored message only. **Not yet wired (documented, not guessed at, same convention #409 itself uses):** `App.tsx`/`TabbedEditor.tsx` don't thread `EditorShell`'s `onQuoteSelection` to `TimelinePanel`'s `incomingQuote`/`ChatMessageCard`'s `onFocusQuote` yet — that connection needs the timeline right rail actually mounted in the running app, which is #409's job, currently unmerged; both ends of the wire exist and are independently tested so that connection is a prop-threading follow-up, not a data-source question. Verified live end-to-end against the real app (D23 walking-skeleton driver, `npm run dev` + `crates/test-driver`) with a temporary `onQuoteSelection` wired in: a real double-click selection made the affordance appear and become clickable (a CSS z-index gap the first live pass caught — `.quote-affordance` needed the same stacked-above-the-doc treatment `.redline-popover` gets, or the CM6 content underneath intercepts the click) and its click round-tripped through the real backend `capture_anchor` command, returning a real anchor with the selected text, correct offset/length, and a real `base_revision` — not a frontend-hand-rolled shape. Five further real, passing tests have a comma in their title so aren't individually listable here (mirrors `NI-undo-policy`'s identical "title has a comma" carve-out): `selectionWatcher.test.ts`'s "reports the offset, length, and text of a non-empty selection" and "never dispatches a transaction of its own (Principle 2: read-only, no caret steal / scroll)", `MessageComposer.test.tsx`'s "sending with no quote attached passes null, not undefined or an empty object", and `ChatMessageCard.test.tsx`'s "clicking the quoted excerpt calls onFocusQuote with the anchor (deep-link back, Principle 6)" and "never calls onFocusQuote on its own — only an explicit click (Principle 2, no focus steal)". **#492 (P2b-7 follow-up) closes the "not yet wired" gap named above**, now that #409 has landed the mounted timeline rail it was waiting on: `editor/TabbedEditor.tsx` forwards `onQuoteSelection` to the *active* tab's `EditorShell` only (mirrors `onChangesDecided`'s identical active-tab-only scoping) and gains a `focusAnchor` on its own imperative handle, delegating to that tab's `EditorShellHandle.focusAnchor` (new). `App.tsx` becomes the quote's owner — a `pendingQuote` state seeded by `onQuoteSelection`, passed to `TimelinePanel` as `incomingQuote`, plus a new `handleFocusQuote` routing `TimelinePanel`'s (also new) `onFocusQuote` prop — forwarded to `ChatMessageCard` exactly like `commentThreads`'s existing pass-through pattern — into `TabbedEditorHandle.focusAnchor`. `EditorShellHandle.focusAnchor` resolves the anchor against the live buffer through the new `commands::document::resolve_anchor` (rust row below) via `editor/ipc.ts`'s `resolveAnchor`, then dispatches a real CM6 selection + `scrollIntoView` — the deep-link-back half of Principle 6 that had no mechanism anywhere in the codebase before this card. Deliberately scoped to TD-6's **exact** mapping only (the live text at the anchor's byte range still equals its `text_snapshot`): a document that moved on since the quote was captured resolves to an honest no-op, never a wrong selection (D17/P2) — snapshot+context fuzzy re-anchor for a genuinely drifted quote is not built here, left alongside `timeline/`'s other still-unwired `onFocusChange` deep-links (`ChangeSetCard`/`CommentThreadCard`) as its own future follow-up rather than folded into this wiring card. Proven end-to-end through the real mounted `App` (`App.test.tsx`'s new "quote-bar wiring" suite): a real editor selection reaches the composer's quote bar, sending carries it into the stream, and clicking the sent quote selects/scrolls the live editor (or is inert when the text has moved). **#2365 (J12 Phase E) fixes the pathless half of this deep-link, deferred from #2360's round-2 review as out of that diff's surface:** `#2304` wired D33's pathless `quoted_selection` shape and the untitled pane's own `Quote to chat` affordance, but `ChatMessageCard.tsx` rendered the back-link `<button>` unconditionally whenever `card.quote` existed — reproduced against #2360's head as `TabbedEditorHandle.focusAnchor` returning `{ landed: false, resolveCalls: 0 }` for an untitled active tab, because `focusAnchor` resolves through `editorHandles`, which only `EditorShell`'s render branch populates; `UntitledDocument` mounts its own `DocumentSurface` under a local ref and is never registered there, so the pathless case can never resolve, not merely after a relaunch. `ChatMessageCard` now branches on `card.quote.documentPath === null`: a pathless quote renders as a plain, non-interactive `<span className="chat-message-card__quote-link">` — the same class the path-bearing quote's `<button>` carries — with no click handler and no cursor affordance. J12 Phase E's own wording — "the quote remains as quoted text and its back-link is absent, not broken — never an affordance that looks live and goes nowhere" — is what this pins; the quote's text is never removed, only the dead affordance. **Round-1 review Important (fixed in this PR, DH-26 clause 2):** the shared-class refactor below moved `font-style: italic` out of the button's own rule and into the lower-specificity shared class while `font: inherit` — a shorthand that also resets `font-style` — stayed on the higher-specificity `button.chat-message-card__quote-link`, so the cascade inverted: the *dead* pathless span kept its italic and the *live* button lost it, exactly backwards from every claim this PR made about the two reading identically. Fixed by replacing the button rule's `font: inherit` with the explicit longhands it actually needs (`font-family`/`font-size`/`font-weight`/`line-height: inherit`), which cannot reset `font-style`; `reads identically as italic quoted text whether the quote is path-bearing or pathless` pins both variants' `fontStyle`/`color` against the real stylesheet (`renderStyled`) and reddens under the pre-fix rule (`button font-style: normal` vs `span font-style: italic`) and again if the branch is forced to always render one variant. **Two remedies were named in #2365 and only the first is built here.** The one shipped (suppress the link) is small and matches Phase E's literal interim wording. The one declined — give `DocumentSurfaceHandle` a `focusAnchor`, forward a handle from `UntitledDocument`, and register it in `TabbedEditor`'s `editorHandles` — is new mechanism, but it would make the round trip genuinely work in-session, which is arguably closer to what Phase E describes when it says "both halves survive" (the relaunch case, where identity survives via #1803). That option is not filed as a card; it is recorded here and in the PR body for CJ to charter if he wants the working version rather than the absent one. **#1803 interacts with this gap but does not by itself close it**: Phase E records that the back-link "becomes durable when #1803 supplies the identity, at which point this clause is superseded rather than amended" — #1803 (merged, #2366) mints durable identity for comments/changes on the untitled pane, but the *mechanism* gap this row fixes (no `editorHandles` registration for `UntitledDocument`) is independent of identity durability and remains, whichever remedy is eventually chosen. |

Wired into `commands::document`'s existing `Document`/`CommandError` shape, registered in `lib.rs`'s `invoke_handler`, and given a D23 test-driver route (`crates/test-driver/src/lib.rs`'s `/capture_anchor`, `tests/http.rs`) so the dev-mode e2e path the frontend's `callBackend` falls back to (no Tauri webview) can reach it too, matching every other document command's convention. P2b-7 follow-up (#492): `resolve_anchor` — takes `content`/`anchor`, slices `content` at the anchor's recorded byte range via `content.get(..)` (bounds- and char-boundary-safe in one call), compares it against `anchor.text_snapshot`, and on an exact match converts the range to UTF-16 through `textops::Utf16Offsets` (the reverse of `capture_anchor`'s own conversion, never hand-rolled). Deliberately only the exact half of TD-6's "exact mapping via stored revision when available, else D17 snapshot+context re-anchor" — a mismatch (the document moved on) is `None`, not a guess. Registered in `lib.rs`'s `invoke_handler` and given the same `/resolve_anchor` test-driver route as `capture_anchor`. |

Every configured path is §3.5-confined (`commands::path_confine::confine`) before use — an escaping value (`..`, an absolute path, a symlink resolving outside) is rejected, not followed, and does not widen a partially-rejected override back out to the whole-tree/default-glob behavior it was meant to replace. A missing config is the ordinary no-override case; an unreadable/malformed file, or an unsupported `workflows.glob` shape (this card only supports `<dir>/*.md`, non-recursive — matching the default's own shape), degrades to the ratified defaults and is reported via `Discovery::skipped` (`SkipReason::ConfigMalformed`/`ConfigPathRejected`) rather than silently ignored (FR-X-5) — a misconfigured repo never looks identical to a well-configured one. An unknown top-level config key is tolerated (forward compatibility); an unknown shape for a key this schema owns is not. |

Its third parameter (`openTabs`) is required, not optional (PR #2360 review round 1, Minor): an omitted `openTabs` silently drops `tabLabel`'s collision ordinal, defeating D33's one-identity-convention guarantee in exactly the case it exists for. **The affordance** — round 1 of this PR found `UntitledDocument.tsx` still a plain `<textarea>` with no quote wiring, correctly declined to build one (duplicating what the then-open #2342 was chartered to build once), and left #2304 open on that blocker. #2342 merged mid-round-2 (`d1a096f89`), giving `UntitledDocument` the shared `DocumentSurface` (TD-24) that already supports `onQuoteSelection`/`QuoteAffordance` generically. This PR then threads `onQuoteSelection` through `UntitledDocument` → `DocumentSurface`, and wires it in `TabbedEditor`'s render loop exactly like `EditorShell`'s own call site, composing `quotedExcerptFor`. `DocumentSurface`'s own conditional rendering (`onQuoteSelection \|\| onCommentSelection`) keeps `Comment` absent on this pane by construction, since `onCommentSelection` is deliberately not passed (`Comments: absent, not zero`, #1803's territory) — matching J12 Phase E's admission of `Quote to chat` and deferral of `Ask agent`/`Comment` exactly. Verified non-vacuous: removing the `onQuoteSelection` prop from `UntitledDocument`'s `<DocumentSurface>` call reddens the render-level test (`QuoteAffordance` stops rendering, so no button to click). **D33 is now fully wired and its ratified route is reachable in production.** |

| FR-CHAT-4 | The `content` snapshot is bounded at 256 KiB (D31): an under-bound dirty buffer still ships whole; an over-bound one is omitted and stated, never truncated; an over-bound untitled document is unreachable and says so; the bound is measured in UTF-8 bytes; (c)/(c-pathless) name the active document in their own text, since the payload does not otherwise determine their subject | vitest | ships content whole at exactly the 256 KiB boundary, withholds content one byte over the 256 KiB boundary\, and states (c) — never a silent truncation, names the active document when a co-listed dirty background tab shares its exact content-less shape (D31\, FR-CHAT-4), an over-bound active untitled document carries no content and no path\, and states (c-pathless) alone — the bound is not waived (D31 clause 5), names the active document when a co-listed background untitled tab shares (c-pathless)'s shape with (b) (D31\, FR-CHAT-4), emits character-identical statements for a just-over-bound buffer and one many times larger — no statement names a size, applies the bound per submission\, not memoized on the tab — a document crossing back under the bound ships content again | present | #2312 implements the bound in `referenceData.ts`'s `entryFor`, measured via the same `utf8ByteLength` helper against `CONTENT_BOUND_BYTES = 256 * 1024` — never `String.length`, a character count, or the JSON-escaped envelope. Over the bound `content` is omitted (absent, never `null`); the withheld fact itself is tracked off the wire in a `BuiltEntry.overBound`, never serialized onto the entry, because (c)'s shape is deliberately identical to an ordinary background entry's (D31) — `statementsFor` reads that flag, not the entry's shape, to choose (c) over (b)/no-statement for a path-bearing entry or (c-pathless) over (b) for a pathless one. **Selecting the right statement is not the same as the statement naming its subject, and round 1 of this PR's own review caught the gap between them**: only the active slot can ever be over bound, which is enough for `statementsFor` to *pick* (c)/(c-pathless) correctly, but the payload still does not determine which listed entry either statement is *about* — (c)'s shape is also every dirty background tab's, and (c-pathless)'s is identical to (b)'s, with which it can co-occur. `OVER_BOUND_STATEMENT` and `OVER_BOUND_PATHLESS_STATEMENT` therefore each open by naming "The active document" in their own fixed text — the "envelope-level text" route FR-CHAT-4 offers over the alternative (a new field on the entry, a schema change) — rather than leaning on selection alone. `names the active document when a co-listed dirty background tab shares its exact content-less shape` and `names the active document when a co-listed background untitled tab shares (c-pathless)'s shape with (b)` are the co-listing coverage this axis needs: each builds a payload holding the over-bound active entry beside another content-less entry of the identical shape and asserts the emitted statement is the one naming "The active document" (the pathless case also asserts (b) rides alongside it, unable to name anything). `applies the bound per submission, not memoized on the tab` proves the obvious wrong optimization (caching the over-bound decision) is absent — the same document ships `content` again on the turn after its snapshot falls back under the bound. Boundary fixtures use a helper (`utf8StringOfByteLength`) built from precomposed two-byte characters, so a `String.length`/UTF-16 measure could not pass by coincidence; an all-ASCII fixture would have passed under every wrong measure. **No statement names a size** — the just-over-bound and much-larger fixtures assert character-identical statement text, and the property J13's "disclose *that* and *which*, never *how much*" rests on. Statement disjointness (exactly one of (a)/(a-pathless)/(c)/(c-pathless) on any entry the requirement gives one to; none on a path-bearing entry withholding `content` for any other reason) remains the merged coverage this row's own `emits exactly one of the two content statements for every entry that carries content` and `gives a dirty background tab identity and unsaved state but never its content` (above) already pin — unchanged by this bound. **`(c-pathless)` stays honest under D32's own co-riding-quote case**: it claims only that no file backs its own entry, never that no route to the document's text exists anywhere in the turn — see the FR-CHAT-3 row's `bounds content and the quote independently`, which proves this bound and the sibling 1 MiB quote bound (D32, implemented in the same PR) never cross-contaminate one turn. **(c)'s parallel wording is reachably false the identical way, and is named here rather than fixed, exactly as D31 named and filed its own unreached limb (Q26/#2249) rather than carrying a stale claim forward silently:** "the file at its `path`, which is the only route the agent has to this document" is not re-examined by this row and is unchanged by D32/D33 or by this round's naming fix — a whole-document quote of that same path can co-ride the turn under D32's own bound and carry the text (c) says is unreachable. Whether this wants its own Q# or folds into an existing card is CJ's call at this PR's sign-off, not this row's. `#2246` implements FR-CHAT-4 as merged, which had no bound, and is correct as built; this row's axes are the bound D31 later added. Filed because neither #2246 nor #2304 covers it (#2304's own `## Not in scope` section declines it explicitly: "D31's and D32's size bounds are separate unimplemented work. Do not fold them in"). |

| FR-CHAT-1, FR-AGENT-1 | The agent's own reply reaches the timeline: the provider's terminal answer is captured off the stream and rides out on the run's `Completed` phase (#1118) | rust | a_successful_result_event_carries_the_turns_reply_text, markdown_and_newlines_in_the_answer_ride_through_verbatim, no_other_stream_event_carries_a_reply, an_unsuccessful_result_event_carries_no_reply, a_blank_or_missing_answer_mints_no_reply, a_turns_reply_text_is_captured_off_the_terminal_result_event, a_turn_that_never_answers_captures_no_reply, a_completed_runs_reply_rides_out_on_its_terminal_phase, a_completed_run_that_never_answered_carries_no_reply | present | #1118: before this, `RunStatus` had no message channel at all — `RunPhase::Completed` carried no payload, so a chat question's answer was discarded at the wire-contract level and the user saw only an activity card. `agent::reply::reply_text` is the pure mapping (terminal `result` event → the turn's answer; nothing else, and never an unsuccessful or blank one); `agent::session` folds it over the stream the way it already folds `session_id`; `agent::run_queue` carries it out on `RunPhase::Completed { reply }`, visible through both transports `RunStatus` serves — `state().last_finished`'s poll and the live `agent://activity` push. #2329: the cells here that assert on the injected `RecordingEmitter` wait on the emitter (`wait_until_emitter_terminal`), not on `wait_until_terminal` — the queue turns terminal before the terminal push lands, so waiting on the queue and asserting on the emitter is a race. FR-X-3's row carries the full account. |

The open-project aggregate now scopes an idempotent persistence fact to the active serialized run; `propose_changes` signals only after the document/change-set write, and `add_comments`/`reply_to_comment` only after their sidecar append. `drain_and_advance` suppresses a completed reply from that actual outcome. `saw_delivery` remains the deliberately wider approximation for a run killed mid-call and J7's stopped/partial card (#1404); this fix does not reinterpret that separate contract. The read-only lane, `permission_request`, and `create_document` remain outside the pending-delivery rule. #2329: the cells here that assert on the injected `RecordingEmitter` wait on the emitter (`wait_until_emitter_terminal`), not on `wait_until_terminal` — the queue turns terminal before the terminal push lands, so waiting on the queue and asserting on the emitter is a race. FR-X-3's row carries the full account. |
| FR-CHAT-1, FR-AGENT-1 | The reply renders as an agent chat card in stream order, once per run, and never for a run that produced no prose (#1118) | vitest | renders a completed run's reply as an agent chat card after that run's activity card, never mints a second reply card when the same terminal status is pushed again, mints no chat card for a run with no prose or one that never completed | present | #1118: `TimelinePanel`'s `appendAgentReply` folds a completed run's `reply` into the stream through the existing `createChatMessageCard("agent", …)` path — which until now had no production caller at all (only `types.test.ts` exercised the agent direction), the frontend half of the same dropped-reply defect. Card id derives from the run id, so a terminal status folded twice (a re-poll, a duplicate push) can never mint a second card for one answer; `stopped`/`failed`/still-running runs and a completed run with no `reply` mint none. Principle 5 ("chat … one chronological stream") in both directions, J2's "normal chat, no special UI". |

CJ's cross-link ruling on #1391 (2026-08-20, from #1725) settled the issue's own open question: the count is derived frontend-side off `RunStatus.activity` — the same list `AgentWorkingCard` already renders — rather than a new backend payload beside `reply`; `agentActivity.ts`'s `distinctFilesRead` counts distinct paths among `"Reading"`-verb lines (the one verb both the `Read` tool and the `get_document_state` MCP tool map to, per `agent::activity`'s own docs), so rereading one file several times still reads one file. `TimelinePanel`'s `appendAgentReply` computes it off the same `status` its working-card fold already has and hands it to `createChatMessageCard`; `ChatMessageCard` renders it as the base card's existing footer slot only when `filesRead > 0` and only for an `agent` message, which resolves the issue's other open question (chip on every reply vs. only grounded ones) without special-casing J2's "agent asks a clarifying question instead of drafting (normal chat, no special UI)" edge state — an ungrounded reply simply reads nothing. `docs/design/` draws no chat card at all (mocks `3a`/`3b` draw only the `You` message and `REVISION` change-set cards), so the chip's *appearance* here reuses the existing `.timeline-card__header` muted small-text convention rather than the lo-fi mock's literal pill/border styling, which is unspecified at hi-fi and raised separately for the Designer to draw (#1391 follow-up, filed and boarded). |

PR #502 review round 4 (Important) closes the other half of that insight: the round-3 fix resolved identity before the write but still wrote to the caller's spelling, and because `store::atomic::write` renames temp-onto-target, a case-variant delivery on NTFS/APFS restamped the user's on-disk filename and stranded the id it had just resolved — leaving the delivery unreachable via `get_document_state` under any spelling. `propose_changes_under_a_case_variant_spelling_leaves_the_filename_and_the_delivery_reachable` (gated Windows/macOS) pins both halves — the filename is unchanged and the delivery still reads back — by asserting through the canonicalizing tool path after the write rather than a raw `docs.json` string lookup, which is the gap that let it past a green suite. |

The chip's colour is unexecuted by this suite (jsdom applies no stylesheet — see `NI-a11y-severity`'s note); `severity.ts` also never renders a blank chip for an empty-string severity (review-gate finding on PR #443). `partial`: this row covers the rendering path only — findings *landing* as comments via the MCP `add_comments`/`reply_to_comment` wiring is the sibling backend row below (P2b-5a, #354). |
| FR-REVIEW-2 | Review findings land as anchored/document-level comments — MCP tool surface | rust | add_comments_mints_one_id_per_comment_and_folds_into_get_document_state, add_comments_captures_a_fresh_anchor_at_the_given_live_offset, add_comments_carries_severity_skill_and_workflow_run_through_the_fold, add_comments_supports_a_document_level_finding_with_no_anchor, add_comments_relocates_a_drifted_anchor_via_fuzzy_reanchor_rather_than_the_wrong_offset, add_comments_orphans_rather_than_drops_or_mislocates_when_the_quote_is_gone, add_comments_attributes_the_comment_to_the_existing_doc_id_despite_a_dot_decorated_spelling, add_comments_attributes_the_comment_to_the_existing_doc_id_despite_a_case_variant_spelling, add_comments_and_reply_to_comment_round_trip_end_to_end, add_comments_supports_a_document_level_finding_over_the_wire, td1_mcp_add_comments_document_level_finding_validates_with_no_anchor, add_comments_rejects_an_anchor_missing_text_snapshot, add_comments_gives_each_comment_in_a_batch_a_distinct_increasing_timestamp, contract_parity_mcp_add_comments_anchor_missing_text_snapshot_rejected | present | P2b-5a (#354): `agent::state::McpContext::add_comments` (TD-1 §3.2, TD-14) — `add_comments(doc, comments[{anchor?, body, severity?, skill?, workflow_run?}])` lands each comment as a new thread through the *existing* `store::comments` event log (severity/skill/workflow_run already supported by the P1-9 fold — wired, not extended). An anchored comment now requires `text_snapshot` (TD-14, PR #439 review loopback, ratified by CJ) and is resolved against the *live* document (never the agent's raw offset blindly, P2): an exact match recaptures a fresh anchor at the live revision (`placement: placed`); a drifted-but-relocatable quote is fuzzy-reanchored (D17, `placement: relocated`); an unrecoverable drift orphans with the quote preserved rather than guessing a position or dropping the comment (`placement: orphaned`). An absent `anchor` lands as this FR's document-level finding (`placement: placed`). Each comment in a batch gets its own strictly increasing timestamp rather than one shared across the call, so the `store::comments` causal fold's `(timestamp, id)` order (P5) never falls back to a random UUID tie-break within the batch (PR #439 review, Minor 3). PR #502 review round 3 (Important, issue #419 follow-through): `add_comments` was still minting/finding its `docs.json` id on `doc`'s raw spelling after `create_document` alone was fixed to normalize — a comment addressed differently than the file's other sidecar activity would have landed under a second, disconnected doc id; `add_comments_attributes_the_comment_to_the_existing_doc_id_despite_a_dot_decorated_spelling`/`..._a_case_variant_spelling` (the latter gated Windows/macOS) pin the fix. |

Hardening `textops::Anchor::new` itself to reject an out-of-range/mid-char range (returning `Result` instead of panicking) is a separate follow-up, not this round. |

round 2\, finding 2), keeps a non-active document's card state across the remount virtualization causes (#1120 round 3) | present | #411: the #350 round-3 review named the defect and its right owner — `TimelinePanel`'s `useState(commentThreads ?? [])` seeded once, and `timeline/CommentCard` mutated only its own `useState(card.thread)` copy, so a resolve/reopen/etc. taken from one card never reached the header's `[Address all… ▾]` open-comment count or any sibling card for the same thread. Fixed by giving `CommentCard` an "owned" mode (`threads` + `onThreadsChange`, both required together — the write half of the pair — else it falls back to its previous private-copy behavior, the exact degrade every other optional prop on this card already follows): it renders the thread it finds in the shared array by id instead of seeding local state, and reports every mutation's full returned thread list up through `onThreadsChange` instead of applying it locally. `TimelinePanel` is the one caller that supplies both, threading its own single `threads` state (already fed by `commentThreads`/re-seeded on identity, and by its own batch-address run) through to every mounted comment card, and bubbling every change back up via its own new `onCommentThreadsChange` prop. `App` is the actual single owner across the whole mounted app (the card's own title): it holds `commentThreads` state, loads it via the existing `comments/ipc.ts::loadCommentThreads` fold whenever the active document changes, and folds `onCommentThreadsChange`'s reports back into that same state — **prop-as-source-of-truth, not a separate global store**, the design choice `timeline/TimelinePanel.tsx`'s own module doc records with its reasoning (matches the `decidedChangeIds`/#410 idiom already established in this exact prop set; `stores/` per tech-design §3.1 is display-state-only, and a plain state/prop pair over the existing IPC fold is exactly that). Knowing *which* document is active needed a new `TabbedEditor.onActivePathChange(path: string \| null)` callback (`editor/TabbedEditor.tsx`) — `onTabsChange` alone under-reports, since switching to an already-open tab via the tab bar mints no new `request` at all; `App` seeds it optimistically from `request` (mirroring `hasOpenTabs`'s own seed) and this callback corrects it, including to `null` on "no tab active," which is what releases a closed/backgrounded document's threads from memory (the previous array is simply replaced, never merged or cached per-path — only the active document's threads are ever held, matching `onChangesDecided`'s identical active-tab-only scoping). Proven at three levels, matching #410's own precedent: `timeline/CommentCard.test.tsx` (owned vs. standalone mode, incl. the FR-CMT-3/FR-CMT-7 `decideLinkedChanges` path — see those rows), `timeline/TimelinePanel.commentOwnership.test.tsx` (the header/sibling-card agreement, mounting `TimelinePanel` directly), and `App.test.tsx`'s new `comment-thread ownership (#411)` suite (a real mounted `App`, a real `loadCommentThreads` round trip, a real `CommentCard` seeded through the existing `loadTranscript` seam #410 already wires, and a real resolve mutation — no second, test-only injection path), plus `editor/TabbedEditor.test.tsx`'s `onActivePathChange` suite for the new callback itself. **Review fix (PR #498 round 1):** `commentPath` (`activeDocumentPath`) advances synchronously on a document switch while `commentThreads` only catches up once the new document's async `loadCommentThreads` resolves, so the header could briefly pair the previous document's still-open threads with the new document's path. `App`'s comment-threads effect now clears `commentThreads` synchronously on every `activeDocumentPath` change (not just the null case) before starting the load, so the header only ever offers a document's own (possibly empty, still-loading) set against its own path. Ratified as **TD-15** (`docs/tech-design.md`): §3.1's `stores/` list is logical, not mandatory, and this App-sited `useState`-over-IPC-fold idiom is the recorded home for this class of display state — `timeline/TimelinePanel.tsx`'s own module doc now cites it directly. **Review fix (PR #498 round 3):** the rebase that brought in #495's editor-anchored comment popover left it unreconciled with this card's single owner — `EditorShell` already accepted `commentThreads`/`onCommentThreadsChanged` (#495) but nothing between it and `App` forwarded either, so a comment created in the editor was written to the sidecar but never reached `App`'s state (the header/timeline stayed stale until a document switch forced a reload), and an owner-loaded thread never highlighted in the editor that opened it. Fixed by threading both through `TabbedEditor` (`editor/TabbedEditor.tsx`'s new `commentThreads`/`onCommentThreadsChanged` props), scoped to the *active* tab only — the identical pattern `onChangesDecided`/`onChangeGenerationChange` already use on the same JSX line. Proven by `editor/TabbedEditor.test.tsx`'s new comment-thread-wiring suite (active-tab-only scoping in both directions, plus a real create through the popover bubbling to `onCommentThreadsChanged`) and `App.test.tsx`'s new editor→owner test (a real mounted `App`, a real editor-anchored popover submit, no test-only injection path) — the same real-mounted-app discipline the rest of this row already follows. **Fix (#1120):** `createCommentCard` (`timeline/types.ts`) had no production caller — every reference was a test — so a created or loaded thread reached this panel's `threads` state (#411, above) and stopped there: nothing ever turned it into the `"comment"` stream card the Comments facet (`facets.ts`, `kinds: ["comment"]`) and Findings facet (provenance-matched off the identical card) select on, so both stayed empty regardless of how many comments existed. Fixed by `foldCommentThreads` (`timeline/TimelinePanel.tsx`, module-private, mirroring `foldRunStatus`'s identical pure-fold-by-stable-key shape one screen up): a new effect folds this panel's own `threads` state into `cards` whenever it changes, minting a card only for a thread with no `"comment"` card here yet (deduped on `thread.id`, never `card.id` or array identity) and leaving an already-materialized thread's card alone — its rendered content still comes from the shared `threads` array via `CommentCard`'s own by-id lookup, not from this card's `.thread` snapshot, so a resolve/reopen/reply reaching it through *either* path never needs a second card. This single fold covers both halves the issue named: a freshly created thread (reaches `threads` through the #411 re-seed effect) and a thread loaded for a document on open (`loadCommentThreads` seeds the identical prop) both fold through the same effect the moment `threads` changes — there is no separate "on load" case. Findings routing requires no new code, exactly as the issue predicted: a materialized card is indistinguishable from any other `"comment"` card, so `facets.ts`'s existing `isFindingProvenance` match already selects it. Guarded on `root`/`commentPath`, the same "no comment surface at all" degrade every other comment-related prop on this panel already follows. Proven directly by the new `timeline/TimelinePanel.commentMaterialize.test.tsx` suite (a card appears with no owner-built seed, is selectable under All/Comments/Findings, and three re-seed shapes — an identical re-seed, an added thread, a content-only mutation — each produce exactly one card per thread, never a duplicate) — and by the full existing `TimelinePanel.commentOwnership.test.tsx`/`TimelinePanel.batchAddress.test.tsx`/`App.test.tsx` suites above continuing to pass unmodified, including the scenarios that seed a comment card through `loadTranscript`'s mock before this fix existed: `foldCommentThreads` dedupes against whatever `"comment"` cards `cards` already holds by `thread.id`, so a card seeded by any other path before the fold runs is left untouched rather than duplicated (the transcript rehydrate itself prepends its history with no thread-id dedup, so a persisted `"comment"` card arriving *after* a fold would double it — unreachable today, since only chat/change-set/batch cards are ever persisted, and now recorded as a deliberate choice in the fold effect's own comment). `App.test.tsx`'s new editor-anchored-popover suite proves the same fix through the real mounted `App` → `TimelinePanel` composition (not just an injected `commentThreads` prop) — a real create through the popover now lands a visible comment card under the Comments facet, confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code with `TimelinePanel.tsx`'s half of this change reverted. **Round-2 review fix (PR #1329):** two Important findings, both rooted in a materialized card outliving the document it belongs to being the active one. Finding 1 — an already-materialized card's `.thread` snapshot never refreshed, so once a document switch cleared the shared `threads` array (`App`'s #498 round-1 clearing effect) `CommentCard`'s `findSelf(threads, id) ?? card.thread` fallback landed on the *original* unresolved snapshot from materialization time, regressing a resolved thread back to "Open" the moment its document stopped being active. Fixed by giving `foldCommentThreads` `foldRunStatus`'s identical update-or-append shape: every pass that still carries a thread refreshes that card's `.thread` in place, so the snapshot is never more than one fold stale. Finding 2 — every mounted comment card was wired to the shared `threads`/`onCommentThreadsChange` pair regardless of its own `card.path`, so a card left over from a document that is no longer active kept the *active* document's write path: acting on it correctly called the backend against its own root/path, but the response flowed back into the active document's shared state, corrupting that document's header count and live editor comment set with a different document's data (the exact #498 pairing, reachable again by this new route). Fixed by gating `commentThreads`/`onCommentThreadsChange` in `renderTimelineCard`/`renderCard` to a card whose `root`/`path` match the panel's own `root`/`commentPath` (a new `activeCommentDocument` option); a non-active card now renders through `CommentCard`'s documented standalone degrade (neither half supplied) instead. That degrade itself needed one more fix layered on top of finding 1's: `CommentCard`'s standalone `ownThread` was seeded via `useState(card.thread)` once at mount and never resynced, so a card flipping from owned to standalone mid-lifecycle (exactly finding 2's new gate) would render *that* stale seed instead of the fold-refreshed `card.thread` finding 1 was keeping current — fixed with a `useEffect` resyncing `ownThread` whenever the `card.thread` prop changes (a no-op while owned, since nothing reads `ownThread` in that branch). Proven directly by two new tests: `TimelinePanel.commentMaterialize.test.tsx`'s "keeps a materialized card's last-known state…" (resolve while active, clear `commentThreads` on a simulated document switch, assert "Resolved" survives) and `TimelinePanel.commentOwnership.test.tsx`'s "does not wire the shared write path…" (switch the active document out from under a mounted card, resolve it, assert the backend call still targets its own root/path but `onCommentThreadsChange` is never invoked) — both confirmed RED against the pre-fix `foldCommentThreads`/`renderCard`/`CommentCard`. **Round-3 review fix (PR #1329):** finding 2's degrade-to-standalone moved a non-active card's rendered state out of panel-owned `cards[].thread` into `CommentCard`'s component-local `useState`, and this stream is virtualized (`VirtualList` renders `items.slice(startIndex, endIndex)`), so scrolling such a card out of the window unmounted it and the remount re-seeded from the untouched snapshot — a thread the user had just resolved rendered "Open" again with live `[Resolve]`/`[Address with AI]` on it (a confidently-wrong position, P2/D17). Fixed by keeping *every* panel-rendered comment card panel-owned: a non-active card is now handed `threads={[card.thread]}` plus an `onThreadsChange` that folds its response back into `cards` through the same `foldCommentThreads` (a new `mint: false` mode — refresh-only, scoped to that card's own `root`/`path`, so it can neither reach the active document's shared set nor materialize the whole of its own document's thread list into the stream). The fold's *dedup* deliberately stays cross-document: during a document switch this panel's `threads` state can still hold the previous document's set for one fold pass after `commentPath` advances (the #498 desync window), which a path-scoped dedup would re-mint under the new path. Round 2's `CommentCard` `ownThread` resync effect is removed with this: nothing flips a panel-rendered card from owned to standalone any more, so `CommentCard` returns to its documented seed-once standalone degrade unchanged. Proven by `TimelinePanel.commentOwnership.test.tsx`'s "keeps a non-active document's card state across the remount virtualization causes" (resolve a non-active document's card against a backend response carrying that document's whole thread list, scroll to the stream's end — asserting the card is genuinely unmounted *and* that the response's other, never-materialized thread minted no card there — then scroll back and assert "Resolved" survives and `onCommentThreadsChange` was still never invoked). Each of the three assertions was proven discriminating by its own mutation: reverting the non-active wiring reddens the "Resolved" one, flipping `mint: false` to `true` reddens the never-minted one, and wiring `onCommentThreadsChange` unconditionally reddens the not-invoked one. |

Every targeted thread takes the *same* event shape the single path uses (both build their event via the shared `address_event`, so single and batch cannot drift), under one shared `run_id` so the delivery can come back as one reviewable grouped set. FR-AGENT-6's partial-completion accounting lives in `run_batch_address`, kept separate from the store so the invariant is directly testable: every targeted id lands in exactly one of `addressed` (landed; stands even if the run died later), `skipped` (ineligible — already resolved or mid-run, never written) or `untouched` (never started), with `interrupted` naming the reason it stopped early. Eligibility is re-checked against the live fold, so a stale caller can never drive an Address event onto a resolved thread. `ThreadWire` now carries `severity`/`skill`/`workflow_run` — the three filter dimensions. J4's overlapping-text edge state needs no new primitive: one change id linked to two threads resolves both on accept, pinned here. **Rendering** the grouped change-set card is the sibling card (P2b-3b); **spawning** the provider run rides the agent-run seam, not a second invocation path. #538 makes that partial-completion path reachable from *outside* the backend: `address_all_with_ai_watching` opens a window between two of the batch's own appends, naming the sidecar being written, and the D23 driver's one-shot `/arm_batch_address_fault` makes that real file read-only inside it — so the append that fails is the real `store::service` write, not a fabricated error (the window returns nothing precisely so it cannot fabricate one). Proven at both levels: directly against the store here, and over http through the driver route the e2e row below drives. **Issue #1778 (ux-design.md §2 "Batch … Working means started, not targeted", UXR-Q10/#1122):** `EventKind::Address` now carries `started` — `true` on the single-thread path (`address_with_ai_marks_the_thread_pending`), `false` on the batch's own per-thread dispatch fallback (`batch_address_targets_every_thread_without_claiming_any_of_them_started` — every target still carries the batch run id, none claims `pending_run_started`). **Per-thread start behind a batch is not built, and no seam for it is invented:** nothing in this build can raise `pending_run_started` for a batch target, so the run-level-only provider — the fallback these rows drive — is the whole of batch behavior today, and the run card is its only progress surface. **A batch with per-thread progress is therefore not covered here, because it does not exist**; it arrives with P2a-15 (#148), which owns the shape of whatever signal it needs. `started`'s `true` value is exercised through the single-thread path only, and `a_batch_target_retains_prior_started_status_without_projecting_either_run` pins the surviving guard non-vacuously: the fold retains the started run and a later batch target independently; the singular `pending_run_started` projection is false while both are pending, so the batch never inherits the first run's Working claim. |

A conforming writer makes that Address event the delivery's `parent_id`, so P5 buffers a clock-skewed or equal-timestamp delivery until its run is applied before attribution and clearing. A re-delegated thread's two deliveries therefore carry distinct attribution even though they share one thread, while a legacy/unpaired delivery degrades to `run_id: None` rather than panicking. `commands::comments::LinkedChangeWire` mirrors the three fields through to the IPC edge, proven over the real command functions (`link_changes`/`address_with_ai`/`decide_linked_changes`), not just the fold. |

| NI-parse-budget-determinism | A decoration assertion never depends on how much of the syntax tree CM6 happened to finish before the assertion ran. `EditorState.create` parses under a 20 ms wall-clock budget (`@codemirror/language`'s `Work.Apply`, measured with `Date.now()`) and `takeTree()`s a *partial* tree when that expires; the remainder is completed by a background scheduler driven by `setTimeout`/`requestIdleCallback` that a synchronous test never pumps. Reading the tree without completing it first therefore makes every structural-decoration assertion load-dependent — the #368 flake. The suite's `ensureParsed` chokepoint completes the parse and republishes it before any assertion reads it | vitest | still decorates the real heading when the 20ms initial-parse budget expired, still reports the heading marker as atomic when the initial-parse budget expired | present | #368: root-caused from a shuffled full-suite run — `structuralDecorations.test.ts`'s "still decorates a real heading that follows the frontmatter" failed intermittently under the full parallel run (one vitest worker per core, all contending) while passing 12/12 in isolation, because its heading node was never parsed. The regression tests inject the starvation through the clock rather than through load (test-plan §4: no sleeps, injectable clock), making a condition that is otherwise only reachable by luck deterministic. Fixing the *reader* rather than the fixture size is what generalizes: `ensureParsed` calls `ensureSyntaxTree` (which advances the parse context) and then applies an empty transaction, because `syntaxTree()` returns the tree the language field last *published* and it only publishes on an applied transaction — the same route the running app takes when background parsing advances. **This guarantee was reader-side and test-only when written here — it did not describe production.** At the time this row was seeded, `structuralInputsChanged` rebuilt only on a doc edit, viewport move, active-line change or reveal/view-mode effect, and CM6's `ParseWorker` published a finished tree with a bare `Language.setState` effect that matched none of those gates. Probed against the real mounted plugin: a starved initial parse rendered 0 decorations, still 0 after the completed tree was published, recovering only on the next edit/scroll/cross-line caret move. That production defect was tracked as #391, filed from this exact probe. It arrived with the decoration layer itself in #153 (PR #215) — not with #216's `StateField`→`ViewPlugin` migration: the merged #153 `StateField` already returned the cached value unless `tr.docChanged` or the active line changed (`5d4a9bf`), and a parse-progress `Language.setState` effect matched neither, so #216 widened an already-blind gate rather than introducing the blindness. **#391 is now fixed** (see `NI-viewport-scoped-decorations` for the mounted-plugin regression test and the `structuralInputsChanged` change itself) — production now rebuilds whenever `syntaxTree()` republishes, closing exactly the gap this row's own docblock had flagged as out of scope. |
