P2a-9 wires it to Settings) and `createSaveRunner` serializes overlapping saves single-flight (latest content wins) so concurrent triggers never race on `store::atomic`'s shared temp — both tested on a fake clock / controllable promise, no sleeps (test-plan §7); `EditorShell` arms the debounce on every edit, flushes a pending save on unmount, and fires the once-per-open normalization trigger (`document.hasNonLfLineEndings`, matched to the backend transform so a lone-CR file is offered the opt-out too) that the P2a-21 banner hooks; `editor/ipc.ts` carries the per-file `normalizeLineEndings` flag on the single save call. P2a-6 (#143) extends `createSaveRunner`'s return type to a per-caller `Promise<boolean>` (correctly correlated with its coalescing — a caller waiting on a request that got coalesced into a trailing write resolves when *that* write settles, never the earlier in-flight one — and carrying that write's success/failure, not just its completion) so `EditorShellHandle.save()`'s imperative save can share this same single-flight serialization instead of racing it with a direct write of its own, and so the FR-ED-3 dirty-close guard can tell a failed save apart from a successful one (review round 1 caught the guard closing — and losing — a dirty tab on a failed save, since the runner's promise had resolved regardless of outcome; fixed by threading the boolean through). **Review round 4 (1 Important, addressed):** the P2a-6 acceptance e2e asserted a tab stays dirty/unsaved across a multi-step interaction while the always-on 1 s autosave could fire mid-scenario — a wall-clock race test-plan §7 forbids. Fixed by making `EditorShell`'s autosave interval an injectable prop (defaulting to `DEFAULT_AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_MS`), threaded `App`→`TabbedEditor`→`EditorShell` and driven by the D23 `setAutosaveIntervalMs` hook (`NI-dev-test-hook`), so the e2e pauses autosave (a large interval) and the dirty window is deterministic rather than raced. **P2a-21 review (1 Important, addressed):** `document.ts` gains `normalizeContentLineEndings` (a pure JS mirror of `textops::normalize_line_endings`) so `EditorShell` can predict the exact bytes a normalizing save wrote without a second backend round trip; the dirty-tracking baseline (`savedContentRef`, compared against the view's own line-separator-fixed serialization) deliberately stays *unnormalized* so a CRLF document doesn't read as permanently dirty right after a successful save — see the FR-ED-7 row for the sibling disk-baseline fix this same finding required. |

Lands alongside P2a-7 (#144, FR-ED-2): the dirty-close guard's imperative save reuses P2a-7's single-flight save runner (extended to return a per-caller `Promise<boolean>`, see the FR-ED-2 vitest row) rather than writing directly, so it can't race an in-flight autosave. **Review round 1 (2 Important, addressed):** (1) a failed guard "Save" was closing the tab anyway — discarding the edit — because the save runner's promise resolved regardless of write outcome; fixed by threading success/failure through `createSaveRunner`/`EditorShellHandle.save()` and aborting the close on `false` (`a failed Save keeps the tab and the guard open rather than discarding the edit`). (2) the session-persist effect could fire before a freshly-restored dirty tab's `EditorShell` had mounted its view, clobbering the just-restored `unsavedContent` with `""`; fixed by falling back to the restore override (not `""`) until `getContent()` reports the view is actually hydrated (`getContent()` now `string | undefined`, `restoring a dirty tab never re-persists it with empty content before its editor has mounted`). Minor: `active_path` is now confined alongside tab paths (`saving_a_session_rejects_an_active_path_escaping_root`), consistent defense-in-depth though not itself exploitable. **Review round 2 (2 Important):** (1) the tab strip was mouse-only — a keyboard user had no way to switch tabs; fixed by giving `TabBar` the WAI-ARIA `tablist` roving-tabindex keyboard contract (`NI-tabbar-keyboard`). (2) the machine-local (gitignored, not TD-4-committed) session-file decision extends TD-12 beyond its recorded lock-file scope — escalated to CJ for conscious acceptance + a tech-design decision-log note at sign-off rather than decided here (reversible if declined; code unchanged). **CJ ruling (escalation resolved):** TD-12's scope extension is ratified as an explicit category, not a one-off (decision-log amendment landed in this card's PR), conditioned on three things also landed here: `docs/formats/tab-session.md` (the on-disk schema doc, FR-X-8), fail-soft restore of a stale/foreign/corrupt `tab-session.json` (`NI-tab-session-fail-soft`, degrading to "no session to restore" rather than crashing or restoring the wrong content — the once-error-returning corrupt-file test is renamed to match the new fail-soft contract), and a regression test pinning that `store::gitignore::ensure_pattern` runs before the session file write even when that write itself fails (mirroring `store::lock::acquire`), closing the "version/timing window" concern. **Still open (separate cards):** undo/redo *policy* (P2a-21 — CM6's own `history()` already runs, wiring beyond that is P2a-21's), inline rename. **Spellcheck landed P2b-9a (#361)** — webview-native over CM6 content, suppressed inside CriticMarkup delimiter and YAML frontmatter ranges (`editor/spellcheck.ts`, wired into `EditorShell`'s extension stack); its tests live on the `NI-spellcheck-suppression` row rather than being re-listed here, since this row's `Test kind` is `rust` and that half is vitest. |
| FR-ED-3 | `File > Open…` opens the picked document as a tab, converging on the existing `request`/`openTab` machinery (#697) | vitest | raises the native picker on a menu fire and opens the chosen path as the active tab, cancelling the native picker leaves state untouched — a clean no-op, refuses a path outside the project with a dismissible shell status — no tab opens, focuses an already-open tab instead of duplicating it (P2a-6 openTab semantics), unsubscribes on unmount so a stale listener cannot outlive the component, a menu fire on the welcome screen (no project open) opens a folder instead of doing nothing | present | #697 closes the P2a plan gap this card's own issue names: no unit ever gave the user a UI route to open a document (`list_documents` is agent-only; the D23 hook is dev-only). `App.tsx`'s new `File > Open…` pipe mirrors the existing delivery/birth pipes exactly — one `subscribeWhileMounted` subscription reacting through `handleOpenDocumentRequest`, which raises the native picker (`stores/menu.ts`'s `pickDocumentFile`) and converges the chosen path onto the *same* `request` state P2a-6 already uses (never a second open path), so "opening an already-open path focuses rather than duplicates" is `TabbedEditor`'s existing `openTab` semantics, asserted here rather than reimplemented. A path outside the open project root is refused (`relativeToProjectRoot` returns `null`, see the `NI-menu-open-document` row), opens no tab, and raises the #722 shell status with the recovery path; only cancelling the native picker remains a clean no-op because the user requested no action. The e2e path stays on the D23 hook (`window.__EMEND_E2E__.openProject`): Playwright cannot drive a native OS dialog, mirroring `onboarding/ipc.ts`'s identical folder-picker gap. **Review of PR #718 (Important):** on the welcome screen (`project === null`, the app's own default state on every fresh launch) the menu-fire subscription previously never mounted at all, so a `Cmd/Ctrl+O` there reached a Rust `emit` with no frontend listener and did *nothing*, silently — the same undefined-behaviour class the out-of-root refusal above was required to decide and state explicitly. Fixed by subscribing unconditionally (not gated on `project`) and routing a fire with no project open onto the same `handleOpenFolder` transition `WelcomeScreen`'s own button already uses — the standard desktop convention for Cmd+O against an empty window, and no new IPC surface (which disabling the menu item would require). Also renamed the unmount-teardown test to what it actually asserts (unmount, not a project-close transition that has no UI surface to test) rather than leave the overclaim standing in this row. |

for the mode the J1 "Display: All/Simple/None/Original ▾" status-bar selector (`editor/DisplayModeControl.tsx`, moved by #2120 under UXR-Q7.2) picks, read by `trackedChanges.ts`'s `buildRedline` — `All` unchanged; `Simple`/`None` render as if every change had been accepted (struck side hidden, underlined side plain); `Original` is the mirror image (reject-all) — matching `textops::display::project`'s text-projection semantics as a pure decoration choice, never a mutation of `state.doc` (the same P1 property the `FR-ED-1`/`P1` rows above pin for source view). `None`/`Original` additionally set `EditorState.readOnly`/`EditorView.editable` (`viewMode.test.ts`) — editing is blocked while either is active, matching the read-only-projection design (§3.3). **#346 — the J1 Phase B pipe.** Both halves existed and neither was connected (nothing emitted a delivery; nothing subscribed). The frontend end: `stores/delivery.ts` subscribes to the backend's landed-delivery event and maps one notice into a `ChangeSetCard`; `App` owns the single subscription and fans it out, parsing the delivery **once** (TD-11 corollary — one authoritative `parse_changes` snapshot per delivery) so the redline and the card can never disagree about what landed; `TabbedEditor` routes it to the tab holding that doc (once per `changeSetId` — re-applying would reseed the snapshot and silently undo triage already done); `EditorShell.applyDelivery` lands text+seed in the one `applyAgentDelivery` transaction and moves the dirty **and** disk baselines, so the tab stays clean and the FR-ED-7 poller never mistakes Emend's own delivery for an outside write (finding M11). The card's changes are joined to the parse **by offset**, not by list position: a delivered document generally carries earlier deliveries' markers too, and listing those would let this card's `[Accept all]` sweep up changes the user never saw in it. Honest degradation throughout: a delivery to a doc with no tab open still raises its card (the set is on disk either way); a failed parse raises neither card nor redline rather than an empty card. **#1827:** a real delivery reaches D23 through the `McpContext` observer, append-only driver log, and shared production frontend pump; the browser acceptance removes the old producer hook. **Runtime prerequisite:** #472 (nothing binds `ProjectMcpServer` in production yet), so this pipe is proven at its seams and by the loopback integration tests, not yet by a running app. |

Display-mode UI / global prev-next nav chrome (P2a-8) stay out of scope. Wiring these entry points to a live CM6 view (P2a-1's scroll target, P2a-5's apply mechanism) is #410 — see the dedicated `FR-ED-4` vitest row below for that seam; a live `propose_changes` delivery creating this card in the first place remains a separate, not-yet-built session/event wiring path. |

| #410: the change-set-scoped seam the #349 round-3 review named as missing — `ChangeSetCard.onDecide` had no production caller, so `[Accept all]`/`[Reject all]`/the type-filtered picks moved only the card's own local counter and touched no text. `editor/trackedChanges.ts` gains `decisionGroupClosure` (the group-closure `decisionsTransaction` sweeps, factored out so it can be reported back without dispatching); `EditorShellHandle` gains `decideChanges(ids, decision)` (dispatches `decisionsTransaction`, returns the full swept id set — ids in, ids out, never a CM6 view crossing the `editor/` boundary, TD-13) and `onChangesDecided` (fires with every id ANY decision entry point drops — this shell's own `decideChanges`, or the pre-existing toolbar/keyboard/popover triage alike, read straight off the transaction's own `dropTrackedChanges` effect rather than a diffed snapshot). `TabbedEditor` becomes a `forwardRef` exposing `TabbedEditorHandle.decideChanges`, routed to the *active* tab only (a background tab's `onChangesDecided` is wired to `undefined`, pinned by its own regression test). `App.tsx` threads `TabbedEditorHandle` and `TimelinePanel`'s new `onChangeSetDecide`/`decidedChangeIds` props together — card→editor and editor→card both cross through it, ids only. `ChangeSetCard` gains `externallyDecidedIds`, reconciled into the same local `decided` set `onDecide`'s return value already feeds (decision reports remain superset-only; the explicit reversal event is the only subtractive path) — the editor→card direction the return-value contract alone can't cover (a decision the card never itself dispatched). `TimelinePanel` accumulates each `decidedChangeIds` batch (a fresh array per event, matching `commentThreads`' own re-seed-on-identity idiom) into that same running set internally, so the accumulation resets on this panel's own remount (`key={project}`) with no second reset path `App` has to keep in lockstep. Both merges (the card's own `decided` set and the panel's `changeSetDecidedIds` accumulator) share one fold, `timeline/decidedIds.ts`'s `mergeDecidedIds` (own `decidedIds.test.ts` suite) — a simplify-pass extraction once the identical superset-merge-with-reference-stability logic showed up in both places, rather than two hand-written copies with nothing keeping them in lockstep. **Known, documented limitation:** this reconciliation set is shared across every mounted change-set card with no per-card document/tab linkage to scope it further — harmless while every card belongs to the one active document (today's only real caller shape, since nothing wires a live `propose_changes` delivery into the app yet), but not a promise against a future multi-document collision; scoping it properly is follow-on work, not silently solved here. The `App.test.tsx` suite mounts a real `EditorView` (via `TabbedEditor`/`EditorShell`) and a change-set card rehydrated through the existing `loadTranscript` seam (P2a-17) — no second, test-only injection path — and drives: `[Accept all]` on the card mutating real document text; the type-filtered overflow menu sweeping in an auto-grouped co-member and reporting the full pair back through `onDecide`; and the editor's own pre-existing toolbar `[✓ Accept]` (bypassing the card entirely) still updating the card's pending count. **P2b-2b (#416):** the change-set-scoped half of the #349 split. Investigated against `main` rather than assumed (mirroring #415's own "already built" finding for the document-scope sibling): the acceptance was already wired — the `onDecide`/`externallyDecidedIds` seam by #410, and the scoping itself by `ChangeSetCard`'s `typeFilterIds`/`decide`, which only ever draw ids from `card.changes`. What was missing was proof, because **every #410 test above mounts exactly one change-set card**, and at one card "scoped to this change set" and "scoped to the document" are indistinguishable. Two tests mounting **two** cards over one document separate them: with no group crossing the card boundary a type-filtered pick leaves the sibling card's own insertion pending (a document-scoped filter would have taken it — both changes are insertions in the same parse), and with a group straddling the two cards the pick sweeps in and decides the co-member regardless, the swept-in half's card retiring it through `externallyDecidedIds` rather than its own (asserted-disabled) controls. That second case is the acceptance's named edge, "P4b group atomicity across the scope boundary" — *Scope note (CJ-ratified 2026-07-21, #416):* **atomicity wins over card scope.** P4b carries no scope qualifier, so this is P4b *applied*, not amended, and requires no `test-plan.md` delta; the user-facing consequence is recorded in `docs/ux-design.md` §2 ("Group closure is not bounded by the change set") and the rule is stated on `ChangeSetCard.tsx`'s `typeFilterIds` and `editor/trackedChanges.ts`'s `decisionsTransaction` doc comments. The affordance UXR-Q5 §2 specifies for that reach (`↳ also decided N linked changes outside this set`) is deliberately **not** part of #416: it belongs to #569 and was gated behind the reversal transition delivered by #680, because without that path the note would outlive the Ctrl+Z that falsifies it. Both #416 tests are characterization/pinning tests over already-shipped behavior (the acceptance's own word is "pin it"), so they passed on first run — the A4 deviation from failing-test-first is recorded rather than silent. **#680 (TD-16 symptom 2b, UXR-Q5 Reversed):** `EditorShell.onChangesReversed` reports the durable ids from the exact `restoreTrackedChanges` inverted effect Ctrl+Z already uses to restore document text and attribution; `TabbedEditor` scopes the callback to the active tab, `App` forwards each fresh batch, and `TimelinePanel` subtracts it from its decided-id accumulator while every mounted `ChangeSetCard` removes the same ids from local state. The card quietly returns to Pending with no badge or residual assertion. The mounted-App tests above use a real `EditorView` and prove both a singleton and every member of a P4b group return together; reverting the subtractive wire leaves the document restored but both cards falsely at 0 pending. |
| FR-ED-4 | Change-set-card triage fails closed on a document/parse mismatch (UXR-Q4, #346/#493): a card carries its own document + parse generation and refuses its triage — disabled, naming its document — rather than deciding ids against a different document or a stale parse (invariants 2 + 4) | vitest | is null before any generation is seeded, reads back the generation a seed carried, survives a decision unchanged — the surviving ids stay decidable against the same parse, moves to the new generation on a reseed and resets to null for a seed carrying none, with no triageTarget prop the card is ungated — it decides exactly as before (legacy callers/tests), a matching document AND generation leaves the card enabled and decides normally, path 1 — a card whose document is not the active tab's is disabled and names its document, path 2 — with no active tab at all (null target) the card is disabled and names its document, path 3 — same document but a stale parse generation (a suppressed dirty-tab delivery) fails closed, fails closed on a delivery to a background tab — the card names that tab and its triage is disabled, fails closed on a delivery to a document with no open tab — the card names it and its triage is disabled, fails closed on a UXR-Q3-suppressed delivery to a dirty active tab — the card is disabled and the unsaved buffer is never decided, re-enables triage after the FR-ED-7 Reload takes a UXR-Q3-suppressed delivery into the buffer | present | **UXR-Q4 (#346, ux-design §5 + J1 Phase B/C, ratified 8d71070a).** Closes the round-4 Critical the rebase surfaced: this card is the first production producer of change-set cards, feeding ids into the #410 triage seam whose precondition (ids from the same parse that seeded the live field) it did not establish — three reachable paths (a delivery to a background tab, to a doc with no tab open, and a UXR-Q3-suppressed dirty-tab delivery) all decided the wrong text, silently, in a protected document. The fix is the cheapest sound correspondence token, not TD-4 stable identity (deferred to #493): the **delivered document's revision** is seeded identically into the live tracked-change field (`editor/trackedChanges.ts`'s new `changeGenerationField`/`changeGeneration`, carried on `seedTrackedChanges`) and onto the card (`stores/delivery.ts`'s `changeSetCardFor`, from `notice.revision`); a document opened fresh seeds its own on-disk revision, so a card persisted-then-reopened against unchanged content still corresponds (its blake3 matches) while every mismatch fails closed. A decision never re-parses, so the generation is stable across triage — the surviving ids stay decidable one at a time. `ChangeSetCard` gains a `doc`/`generation` pair (the `CommentCard.root`/`path` pattern one door down) and a `triageTarget` prop: on a mismatch (different document, stale generation, or no active tab) every triage control disables and the card names `card.doc` (`.change-set-card__mismatch`, `role="status"`) — a visible degraded state, never a silent no-op or a silent wrong decide. The same gate refuses to absorb `externallyDecidedIds` while mismatched, so the card stays frozen at its last verifiable counts and provenance as UXR-Q5 rule 2 requires (#493). `EditorShell`→`TabbedEditor`→`App` lift the active tab's live generation into a `triageTarget`, and `App.handleChangeSetDecide` re-checks the live generation against it before routing (the belt to the card's render-time suspenders). This closes the safety axis of the #410 row's "no per-card document/tab linkage" limitation — a mismatch is now an honest refusal; cross-document routing and re-focus (so the user could *act* on a mismatched card by switching to its document) are the deferred #493 work. **Review round 5 (1 Important, addressed) — the degraded state must be escapable and must name the way out.** Two halves of one defect in the fail-closed state this row creates. (a) The mismatch copy was unconditional ("open it to review them"), which instructs the impossible on path 3: the UXR-Q3-suppressed delivery's document *is* the active tab, open and on screen. The copy now branches — a stale parse of the document already on screen names the FR-ED-7 reload that actually resolves it ("these changes are saved to `doc` — this tab doesn't match them, so reload the file"), a different/absent document keeps "open it" (FR-X-5: a degraded state is honest about the way out, not just about being degraded). (b) `handleReload` seeded no generation, so the ratified UXR-Q3 recovery path *closed the state permanently*: reload put exactly the delivered bytes and their parse into the buffer, yet the field's generation resolved `null`, the card stayed mismatched, and FR-ED-4's three triage entry points were dead for that delivery forever. Fixed with data the poller already fetched and discarded — `openDocument` returns `{content, revision}`, so `externalChange` now carries `diskRevision` and the reload seed uses it as the generation. Triage re-enables exactly when it is provably safe (the buffer holds that revision's parse), and stays closed otherwise. The `ChangeSetCard`/`trackedChanges` cases prove the card + token in isolation; the `App.test.tsx` cases drive the three paths end-to-end through a real `TabbedEditor`/`EditorShell` and the same `parseChanges` round trip, proving they are *reachable* and fail closed, not just theoretically guarded. |
| FR-ED-4 | UXR-Q5 acting-card reach note (#569): a change-set-scoped decision reports linked changes decided outside its own set and withdraws the claim on reversal | vitest | a type-filtered pick on one change-set card leaves an unrelated card's own insertion untouched (#416), a type-filtered pick sweeps in a group co-member that belongs to a DIFFERENT change-set card — P4b atomicity wins over card scope (#416), a mismatched card suppresses a reach note it cannot verify (UXR-Q5), renders a plural reach outcome as a status line, retains acting-card reach across a virtualized unmount and remount, withdraws reach on reversal and restores it when redo re-decides the reached id, does not restore historical reach when another surface re-decides the same ids | present | Mounted `App` + real `EditorView` proof over the production #410 decision seam: an ordinary two-card type-filtered decision renders no reach note; a P4b closure containing one acting-card id and one sibling-card id renders `↳ also decided 1 linked change outside this set` only in the acting card's expanded body, with no link or control. `TimelinePanel` retains outside-set provenance per acting card so virtualization cannot erase the accounting. #680's reversal batch removes the ids from the current decided set, which withdraws the note while leaving its dormant attribution available to an editor redo; if another card later claims the restored group through its own action, `handleChangeSetDecide` transfers those ids away from every other card's self/reach account so the historical reach stays withdrawn (UXR-Q5 Reversed, J1 Phase C, FR-X-5, P4b). | FR-ED-4 | UXR-Q5 decided-elsewhere provenance (#695/#798): a card persistently reports its own changes decided by another surface and withdraws the claim on reversal | vitest | renders decided-elsewhere provenance as a persistent non-interactive status line, a later render with an empty/omitted batch never un-decides an id already accumulated, Accept all on a mounted change-set card changes the live document text, deciding a change directly in the editor (not via the card) keeps the card's pending count honest too, Ctrl+Z after a decision restores the change-set card to Pending with no residue (UXR-Q5 Reversed), an in-editor decision after undo is reported as decided elsewhere instead of restored self-attribution (#798), a type-filtered pick sweeps in a group co-member that belongs to a DIFFERENT change-set card — P4b atomicity wins over card scope (#416), does not restore historical reach when another surface re-decides the same ids, a mismatched card suppresses a reach note it cannot verify (UXR-Q5) | present | Mounted `App` + real `EditorView` coverage proves the UXR-Q5/J1 Phase C provenance split over the production #410 seam: a card action records its own settled ids and renders no extra line; in-editor triage renders `↳ N changes decided elsewhere`; and a cross-card P4b sweep renders the acting card's #569 reach note while the swept sibling reports only its own decided-elsewhere count, so one event is not double-counted on either card. The singleton card path covers both post-reversal branches: redo restores the acting card's own attribution and therefore renders no false decided-elsewhere line, while a fresh toolbar decision clears that dormant attribution and renders `↳ 1 change decided elsewhere`. `EditorShell` tags forward decisions as `card` or `editor` and recognizes CM6's history `redo` user event; `App` threads that source with the ids so `TimelinePanel` can choose correctly without owning editor history. After reversal, a foreign card action deciding the same P4b group transfers attribution, leaves the original card's reach withdrawn, and classifies its own change as decided elsewhere. `TimelinePanel` keeps self-decision and reach attribution durable beside the current decided-id accumulator; visibility is their intersection, so reversal returns the card to Pending with no residue while redo can restore honest provenance, a fresh editor decision clears dormant attribution, and an explicit later card action transfers ownership away from historical accounts. `ChangeSetCard` renders the count only in the expanded body as a non-interactive `role="status"` and suppresses it under UXR-Q4 mismatch, satisfying FR-X-5 without promising dead navigation. |

The regression drives accept → reverse → accept again → unmount/remount and proves the stale reversal cannot return the re-decided change to Pending. The mounted-App Ctrl+Z case additionally proves undo withdraws the derived fully-decided collapse without an explicit Expand override and redo restores the collapsed decided state. |

unmoved baseline, does not arm autosave — nothing was written to the buffer to save back | present | P2a-21 (#156): `editor/externalChange.ts` (new) is the detection mechanism — `isExternallyChanged` (pure content compare) plus `createExternalChangePoller`, an injectable-interval, never-overlapping poll (test-plan §7 "injectable clock", mirroring `autosave.ts`'s scheduler shape). Reuses `open_document` (already a pure, side-effect-free read, §3.1) rather than standing up a new native FS-watcher-to-frontend push surface — that push mechanism (`store::watch.rs`'s P2a-10 `notify`-based watcher generalized beyond `.emend/`, plus Tauri event emission and D23 test-driver push parity) is real follow-on infrastructure work, not yet built anywhere in the app (confirmed: `SidecarWatcher` has no caller today), so a bounded-interval poll off the existing read command is the scoped, testable mechanism landing here; a conscious, documented trade-off, not a silent shortcut. `editor/ExternalChangeBanner.tsx` + `editor/ExternalChangeCompare.tsx` (new) are the ux-design.md line 417 Reload/Keep mine/Compare banner (non-modal `role="status"`, never a dialog) and the v1 side-by-side Compare view (read-only, no three-way merge — ux-design's explicit YAGNI call); `EditorShell.tsx` wires the poll, shows the banner on divergence (skipping a disk state the user already dismissed via "Keep mine"), Reload replaces the CM6 doc + moves the dirty baseline + reseeds tracked changes, and Keep mine is the ARIA-highlighted default exactly when the tab is dirty (never silently overwrite either side — the acceptance line, verified by the highlighted-default vitest case above). `EditorShellHandle`/`EditorShellProps` gain an injectable `externalChangePollMs`, threaded the same way `autosaveIntervalMs` already is. **Review round 1 (1 Important, addressed):** the poll compared disk bytes against `savedContentRef` (the dirty baseline, kept in the view's own pre-normalization line endings) — for a CRLF file, the very first normalizing autosave wrote LF bytes to disk while that baseline stayed CRLF, so the next poll always saw a "divergence" and fired the banner against the file's own save, worst on the primary Windows CRLF path. Fixed with a second, separate baseline: `diskContentRef` mirrors the bytes a normalizing save actually wrote (`document.normalizeContentLineEndings`) and is what the poll compares against, while `savedContentRef` stays untouched so `dirtyTrackingExtension` (a different consumer of the same ref before this fix) isn't broken by the same change — see the FR-ED-2 row for that sibling regression test. **Review round 2 (1 Important, addressed):** `ExternalChangeBanner.tsx` used `autoFocus` to make the safe action the keyboard default, but the banner mounts from the background poll — `autoFocus` seized keyboard focus the instant the poll fired, a focus steal that contradicts ux-design Principle 2 (line 16) and the J1 edge state (line 132). Removed both `autoFocus` props; the visual `--default` highlight (already the acceptance the tests assert) makes the safe choice discoverable without moving focus, and the buttons stay Tab-reachable. New regression test pins that neither dirty nor clean banner moves `document.activeElement` on mount. **#368 (flake root-cause):** the CRLF-save test's mocks queued the post-save LF read up front, asserting a disk state that could not exist yet — any poll tick in the 10 ms window before `save()` compared LF against the CRLF baseline and tripped the very banner the test asserts is absent (a race, not a behavior). The mocks now model the *disk* as state the save mutates, so no poll/save interleaving can lie, and a committed companion test forces the pre-save poll tick explicitly — it fails against the old queued-sequence shape, which is what stops that shape returning. **#1701 (deferred by #1690's own reviewer, DH-26 clause 2):** the same swallow one layer up — `checkDiskForExternalChange`'s unguarded tail (everything after the supersession guard) had no `try`/`catch` at all, so a throw there (e.g. reading `disk.content` off a payload that cannot answer) escaped through `createExternalChangePoller`'s `void check().finally(...)` as the same unhandled rejection #1690's fixture defect relied on to be caught at all — loud to vitest, silent to the app, and reported nowhere even with a real `onError` consumer wired. The tail now carries its own guarded `try`/`catch`: with an `onError` consumer it reports once per run of consecutive failures via a dedicated `checkFailureReportedRef` (reset whenever a check completes normally, not scoped to a Missing episode the way `pauseReadFailureReportedRef` is, since a tail throw has no episode to belong to) rather than on every tick; with none it re-throws, deliberately left to escape exactly as `externalChange.ts`'s own module doc now states — the two new cases above pin "once, not every tick" and "re-arms after a successful check between failures." **Review round 1 (2 Important, addressed):** both gaps were proven live by execution, not merely asserted. The escape itself was unpinned — mutating `if (!onErrorRef.current) throw error;` to `return;`, the exact swallow-tidy this card forbids, left the suite fully green — because every existing case that awaits the poller's own `.finally()` chain can only ever *observe* an escaped rejection as an unhandled one, never assert on it in-process; awaiting it directly would consume the very rejection the property is about. Fixed with a capturing partial mock of `./externalChange` (the same `importActual` wrapper `./selectionWatcher` already uses at the top of this file) that hands a test the exact `check` callback the poller was constructed with, so a direct call's rejection can be asserted with `.rejects.toThrow(...)` — consumed deliberately, emitting no unhandled rejection of its own — while still failing under the swallow mutation. Only the narrower claim, "the poller itself never `.catch()`s", remains genuinely untestable in-process, and is carried solely by `externalChange.ts`'s own doc comment. Separately, deleting the per-document reset at the mount effect's `checkFailureReportedRef.current = false;` also left the suite green: a stale `true` carried from a prior document into the next would make that next document's *first* tail failure neither reported nor re-thrown — a total silent swallow, precisely what the card forbids. Pinned with a new case modeled on the existing per-document-reset precedent ("leaves no part of one document's pause behind for the next document"), extended to this ref: document A's first failure reports once, then swapping to document B (a distinct `onError` call count target) proves B's own first failure reports independently rather than being suppressed by A's already-set flag. **UXR-Q3 (#346, ux-design §5 + J1 Phase B edge state — "suppress + banner", ratified 3505fb6f):** an agent delivery may only land in the buffer when the tab is clean; on a *dirty* tab `EditorShell.applyDelivery` applies nothing and moves neither baseline (M11's clean-tab attribution still moves both). The delivered set is already on disk/sidecar (and its change-set card still reaches the timeline), so the unmoved `diskContentRef` lets this same FR-ED-7 poll surface it as an external change — Keep mine the highlighted default — rather than silently overwriting the user's unsaved edit. The four vitest cases above pin buffer-survival, that the tab stays dirty, the banner path firing, and that no autosave is armed; the fix makes the loss *impossible*, not merely undo-recoverable (review note). **#346 review round 5 (1 Important, addressed):** Reload is the ratified way *out* of that suppression, so it must restore the delivery's triage as well as its text. It seeded no UXR-Q4 generation, leaving the reloaded buffer holding the delivered parse while the change-set card could never prove it — permanently untriageable on the one recovery path UXR-Q3 exists to provide. The banner state now carries `diskRevision` alongside `diskContent` (the revision `openDocument` already returned and the poller discarded) and the reload seed uses it as the field's generation. Threading it required no new round trip and no new mechanism. The D23 hook gains `setExternalChangePollMs` (the `NI-dev-test-hook` / `setAutosaveIntervalMs` seam, one door down) so the App-level reload-after-suppression case drives the poll on an injected interval rather than waiting out the 5 s default — test-plan §7, no sleeps. **#1690 (flake root-cause, the required Windows gate):** #368's lesson recurred on the other side of the same seam. The poll rearms after every read settles and keeps reading for as long as the tab is mounted, so nothing a test awaits bounds how many reads it takes — a contended runner takes more of them than a quiet one. Two fixtures assumed a bound. `EditorShell.test.tsx`'s `openDocumentMock` was a bare `vi.fn()`, so a read past the queued answers returned `undefined` — a value `openDocument` (`Promise<DocumentPayload>`) cannot return — and `checkDiskForExternalChange` read `.content` off it inside a poll nobody awaits, an unhandled rejection surfacing after the owning test had already passed; the run reported 1315/1315 passed and still exited 1 (PR #1684, `8d538d556`, attempt 1 red / attempt 2 green at the same commit). The mock now defaults to a read that has not come back yet, which is inert and cannot be mistaken for disk bytes, and the new companion case above forces an unqueued read deterministically and pins that the fake answered it with a value `openDocument` could actually return. Separately, "retires a read taken during the episode when Recreate publishes the bytes itself" asserted an exact read total against that same self-rearming poll, and did so in three racing ways at once. It captured the total after merely "more than one read" — a condition the mount's own reads already satisfied, so the count could be taken while one of those was still in flight; it then asserted that total *after* releasing the held read, which rearms the poll onto a `mockReturnValue` that is already settled, so the poller spins a fresh read every millisecond straight into the assertion window; and the banner assertion beside it raced those same reads, whose stale pre-Recreate bytes would raise the very banner it asserts is absent. The case now waits for the tick that actually takes the held read, asserts the count immediately after the Missing exit — the moment the property is about, namely that `created` starts no read of its own — rather than after the settle, and parks the poll on a second held read before releasing the first, so the banner assertion has no live poll to race. **Measured, not asserted:** with an identical 25 ms stall applied to both arms and alternated under one sustained load, the pre-fix file failed **6/6** runs (19–21 unhandled `TypeError`s each, every test still reporting passed — the exact gate signature) against **0/6** post-fix; unamplified, the pre-fix file reproduced the `TypeError` spontaneously **1 run in 32** at load average 75–97, vitest naming the same test the CI run named. The count case received 3 reads where it expected 2 at a 60 ms poll interval before the fix and passes at 1/5/20/60/120 ms after it, and stays non-vacuous — it fails `expected 6 to be 4` when `recreateMissingDocument` is made to answer `reappeared` (an exit that does read) instead of `created`. The first attempt at this case fixed only the capture condition and was **insufficient**: it failed `expected 6 to be 4` once in 10 loaded runs, which is what surfaced the settle-rearm leg above — a reminder that a race fix is a claim to re-measure under load, not to reason to. No product code changed. |
| FR-ED-7 | After Emend captures an ordinary changed-on-disk side, an undecided banner issues no further write until the author chooses | vitest | cancels a queued trailing write and resolves its waiters false before publication (#1599), does not let a later edit autosave over disk while the external-change choice is undecided (#1599), does not flush the pending autosave over divergent disk when the editor unmounts (#1599), manual Save writes nothing and returns attention to Keep mine while the choice is undecided (#1599), imperative Save reports refusal and returns attention while the choice is undecided (#1599), Keep mine immediately saves edits accumulated while autosave was paused (#1599), does not launch a queued trailing save after divergence is detected while the first save settles (#1599), an already-issued completion leaves the captured banner and Compare side intact (#1599), Reload recovers the captured disk bytes after an already-issued save publishes (#1599), ignores issued A but advances the captured side from external C to later external D (#1599), Keep mine is the sole authorized next write after an already-issued save publishes (#1599), does not let an issued completion from a prior document identity poison the next disk baseline (#1599), still writes a trailing edit queued behind an in-flight save when the editor unmounts with no divergence (#1599), Save returns from the dirty-close guard to an unresolved external-change choice without writing | present | #1599 / FR-ED-2 / FR-ED-3 / FR-ED-7 / ux-design “Document integrity & lifecycle moments”; CJ architecture amendments 2026-08-16. The EditorShell cases model one shared disk state through both `openDocument` and `saveDocument`, invoke the scheduler callback directly at its deterministic clock seam, and cover unmount, queued-runner, already-issued publication, current-external-revision, and cross-document identity barriers without sleeps. Detection increments an authorization epoch and cancels scheduler/runner-owned trailing work. An issued payload is the only disk revision ignored as Emend's own publication; a later genuinely external revision updates the process-lifetime captured side used by banner/Compare/Reload/Keep mine. A save issued under the older epoch may publish under detection-bound v1, but its stale completion cannot move the dirty baseline or dismiss the choice. It does advance the disk-only known-publication baseline inside the same live document, so a poll held between that completion and Keep mine's authorized write does not mistake Emend's own atomic output for a new external side; an unmounted document's completion cannot advance its successor's baseline, even when their bytes coincide. Reload replaces the buffer from the current capture and immediately submits those bytes through the atomic save runner, leaving the buffer dirty until persistence succeeds; its autosave-off close/reopen barrier proves the chosen recovery side is durable without a later automatic save. The pure runner case proves suppressed waiters resolve false. Manual, imperative, and dirty-close Save return attention without writing; Keep mine alone submits the accumulated live buffer as the next authorized ordinary-path write. Save As remains unchanged and writes only the separately chosen identity. Captured bytes are process-lifetime recovery data, not durable recovery across process loss. **Review round 1 (1 Important, addressed):** the unmount cleanup ran `scheduler?.flush()` immediately followed by `runSave.cancelTrailing()` — when an ordinary save was already in flight at teardown, the flush's request coalesced into that runner's `trailing` slot and the very next line cancelled it, silently dropping the final edits on a document with no divergence at all (exceeding what FR-ED-7/ux-design scope to "while the choice is unresolved"). The unconditional cancel was compensating for a real but separate bug: `ownPublicationRef` was a component-level ref shared across every `[root, path]` remount rather than scoped like every other save/poll participant (`cancelled`, `runSave`, `scheduler`, `checkDiskForExternalChange`), so a trailing write inherited from a prior document identity could briefly overwrite it after the successor document's own reset had already run, letting that successor's poll misread the stale publication as its own and suppress a genuine external change. Fix: `ownPublication` is now a plain closure-local to the mount effect, needing no explicit reset and making the cross-document leak structurally impossible; the unmount `cancelTrailing()` is removed since it is no longer protecting anything the divergent path doesn't already handle at detection time (`scheduler?.cancel()` + `runSave.cancelTrailing()`, unconditional on divergence). New cell pins the previously-lost case directly: two edits, the first save in flight, the second coalesced to trailing, unmount, then release — the trailing write must still land. All thirteen prior cells continue to pass unchanged. |
| FR-ED-7 | Reload synchronization across React and CodeMirror (#1541) | vitest | Reload replaces the buffer with the disk content and the banner clears | present | The live `EditorState` is the synchronous completion boundary; the regression awaits CodeMirror's separate DOM projection before asserting the visible text, rather than treating the React banner's disappearance as a proxy for CM6 paint. A pre-fix isolated stress loop reproduced the Windows signature while the imperative handle already reported the disk revision, proving a harness synchronization race rather than stale editor state. |
| FR-ED-7 | Writes stay paused across a Missing reappearance, so a concurrent creator's bytes survive | vitest | never lets an autosave armed while Missing write once the path is occupied again, keeps writes paused across a Recreate that reappeared\, until its own read has landed, runs an edit made during that read once it proves the file came back as Emend knew it, drops an edit made during that read rather than writing it over a concurrent creator, holds the edit made while Missing even when the file came back exactly as it was, does not let a read taken while the document is still Missing resume its writes, resumes autosave after a Recreate created the file even if the path cannot be read, ignores a pre-Missing read that returns after the current reappearance read started, invalidates a pre-Missing read when Recreate publishes the current bytes without another read, retires a read taken during the episode when Recreate publishes the bytes itself, lets only the newest overlapping direct reappearance read settle the episode, does not carry a Keep-mine dismissal across the loss of the path's identity, ends an open Compare when the file it was comparing vanishes, does not offer the vanished file's version back once the path returns, refuses the close guard's save over the divergence its own read just raised, says so when the read that would lift the pause cannot complete, does not lift the write pause when the settling read succeeds but its tail throws (#1701), ref.save() resolves false rather than rejecting when the settling poll's own tail throws (#1701), leaves no part of one document's pause behind for the next document | present | #1595 (P0 data loss): J2's write pause was only `missingRef` consulted at save time, so ending Missing resumed writes at once — with the debounce armed while the file was gone still pending, and with nothing having read what had taken the path over. Whichever write went first destroyed a concurrent creator's bytes: the disk side this requirement exists to preserve, and the source of truth TD-2 makes the file. **Measured, not inherited:** the reported failure named the `reappeared` branch of `handleRecreate` calling `onMissingResolved()` before its FR-ED-7 check completes, but the same loss reproduces on the automatic-reappearance route where neither runs — so the pause, not that branch's await order, is the defect. The pause now outlasts the missing state: raised on entering Missing, lifted only by a completed read that establishes the disk side, or by a `Recreate` that published bytes Emend chose under TD-18 confinement (which needs no read — the fifth test above is the over-blocking guard, and is the one case that passes against the pre-fix head). Leaving Missing also drops the debounce armed while the file was gone and reads the path immediately rather than an interval later. An automatic save inside the pause defers and runs only if that read found nothing to preserve; a divergence drops it, since writing then is the silent overwrite this requirement forbids. An explicit save (Cmd/Ctrl+S, the close guard's) waits the read out instead — it is the user's own act — and reports `false` only when no write was issued at all (FR-X-5). Deterministic by construction (test-plan §7): the read is held on a barrier the test settles by hand and the debounce runs on a fake clock, so no sleep and no interval decides the ordering. Every one of the seven production lines this repair adds is discriminated by a named cell, proven per assertion rather than as a conjunction: raising the pause on entering Missing (6 cells), the `triggerSave` refusal (2), dropping the debounce on leaving (2), the immediate read on leaving (3), `resumeWrites` refusing to lift while still missing (1), a divergence dropping the deferred save (1), and the `created` branch clearing the pause (1). The first sweep found three of those lines covered by no assertion at all — the two dedicated cells and the unreadable-path variant above exist because of it, and the earlier "resumes autosave with no read at all" case was replaced because the poll rescued the pause and it pinned no such thing. A second round (CJ's cells, extended with the `created`-path case) closed a further hole the first shape left open: a read *already in flight* when the file vanished could land after it came back and settle the episode with pre-deletion bytes — resuming writes over whatever now occupies the path, the same loss by a longer route. Every read now carries the generation it was issued in and may only speak if nothing has moved it since; a `Recreate` that publishes the bytes itself moves it too, being the newest fact about the path with no read to supersede the outstanding one. Both lines are discriminated individually (5 cells and 2 cells respectively). **Review round 1** then found the generation incomplete in the one place no cell in this file can reach: entering Missing did not retire the reads already in flight, so one issued *before* the file vanished could land in the gap between the commit that ends Missing and the effect that acts on it — clearing the pause, which is the flag that effect's debounce-cancel and immediate read are both gated on. That gap does not exist under `act`, which flushes a commit and its passive effects together, so its cell lives in `EditorShell.scheduling.test.tsx` on React's own scheduling; it reddens for that one line and nothing else, 3/3. The same round added the explicit-save refusal (an explicit save must not overwrite the divergence its own read just raised — the close guard's user is answering "save before closing?", not "overwrite the stranger's file"), the per-document reset of this state on (re)mount, the FR-X-5 report when the settling read cannot complete, and the clearing of a "Keep mine" dismissal whose file no longer exists. Each is discriminated by its own cell. **Review round 2** closed the last gap in that block: the entry-time `setExternalChange(null)` / `setComparing(false)` had no cell, and they are not cosmetic — a `[Compare]` left open when the file vanishes suppresses J2's Missing banner entirely (the banner renders only while nothing is comparing), and a divergence left standing returns with the path and offers `[Reload]` of a version belonging to a file that no longer exists. Both now redden their own cell and only their own. The third clear beside them, the undecided-divergence mirror, remains disclosed sibling state with no cell of its own. Six of the original seven cells also fail against the pre-fix head, each on `save_document` being called; the seventh is the over-blocking guard, which must pass at both heads and does. **#1699 keeps this deterministic clock cheap in wall time:** the Keep-mine cell parks any later poll on the fixture's never-settled read after its reappearance read is already in flight, so advancing 5,000 ms makes the 1,000 ms autosave due without executing 5,000 already-settled 1 ms poll turns. Its explicit bound allows zero or one post-settle poll; restoring the permanent settled barrier produces 5,006 total reads and reddens that assertion. **#1701:** the settling read's own tail (not the `openDocument` call the FR-X-5 case above already covers) could itself throw — e.g. accessing `disk.content` on a payload that cannot answer — and nothing downstream of the supersession guard was ever wrapped in `try`/`catch`, so a throw there both skipped `resumeWrites` (the pause correctly stays up, proven directly here) and, with no `onError` consumer, rejected the very promise `saveNow` was awaiting via `pollNowRef.current()`, which would have rejected `saveNow()` and `ref.save()` in turn — a caller the tab layer's dirty-close guard (`TabbedEditor.tsx`) awaits with no `try`/`catch` of its own, leaving that guard neither closing nor reporting. `saveNow` now wraps its own `pollNowRef.current()` await in `try`/`catch`, falling through to the existing `if (diskStateUnknownRef.current) return false` refusal it already had for the ordinary read-failure case. |

Drives J2's second reappearance clause ("differing content transitions to the ordinary changed-contents banner ... preserving both sides"), which the suite drove only for identical content. Cross-platform: the reappearance is the ordinary rail refresh, so it runs everywhere rather than only where TD-18 confinement offers `[Recreate]`. The two D23 clocks fix the ordering instead of waiting for it — a 2s debounce still pending when the path returns tens of milliseconds later, and a 60s poll so only the transition itself can read the file. The banner is the oracle in both directions: reaching it proves a write did not land first (one that had would have moved Emend's own baseline onto the disk, leaving no divergence to report), and against the pre-fix head it never appears at all, because nothing reads the path at the transition — divergence handling is never installed, which is AC2's own failure. A dirty local edit is held off disk by the write-pause itself while an external write is captured; every further FR-ED-7 poll read is then held (D23 transport hold, test-plan §7b) so a *second* external write can land on disk without being re-captured as the banner's side — pinning the captured side to the first write for the rest of the test. Clicking `[Reload]` is then asserted along the real path rather than by a single final byte compare: the outgoing `save_document` request's own JSON body is read off the wire and matched against the captured (first) bytes, not the second write now actually on disk; the tab's `data-dirty` attribute is asserted `true` and the disk is asserted still holding the *second* write's bytes while that request is held in flight (the Rust command itself still runs once released — nothing here is mocked); and release fails into the Reject-colored status line; only a real Try-again write then clears `data-dirty`, restores ordinary Saved, and confirms the captured bytes replaced the second write. That transition — disk moving from the second write's bytes to the captured bytes only after release — is what makes the final read evidence of the recovery write specifically, rather than a coincidental match with bytes already there before the click (the two would otherwise be indistinguishable, since the first external write already left the captured bytes on disk before Reload was ever clicked). Mutation-adjudicated (PR #1640 body): a frontend mutation substituting empty content for the captured bytes reddens the `save_document` payload assertion at that exact line; a backend mutation disabling `atomic::write` inside `write_document_to` (`save_document` still returns success) reddens only the final disk-read assertion, at `expect.poll(() => project.readSaved()).toBe(captured)`, leaving the request-payload and dirty-flag assertions green. |

A failed write (11c.5, CJ-ratified 2026-08-28 on #1641) keeps the reloaded buffer verbatim, stays dirty, and re-extends the same write pause that guards the ordinary observed episode (`triggerSave`/`saveNow` both gate on `recoveryPhaseRef.current !== null`) — proven by firing autosave and calling `ref.save()` after a failure and asserting `saveDocumentMock`'s call count does not move. `Try again` stays inside the `failed` state (toggling `retrying`) rather than re-entering `reloading`/`pending`, since a retry brings in nothing new from disk — reusing Reload's own progress copy for it would be dishonest; the DOM's own `disabled` attribute is what bounds it to one in-flight attempt (a second click while retrying lands on an inert button). `Save As…` and `Compare` reuse the existing `runSaveAs`/`ExternalChangeCompare` paths unchanged; the failed phase carries its own captured `diskContent` so Compare still has an "on disk" side once the ordinary `externalChange` episode itself has already resolved (at the moment Reload was clicked, not when the recovery write settles). The correct axis, per the reviewer's own framing, is *which bytes* are suppressed, not *whether detection runs*: `handleReload` now sets `dismissedDiskContentRef.current` to the captured bytes (the same mechanism Keep mine already uses) instead of nulling it, and the poll's `diverged` predicate no longer special-cases the recovery phase at all — a genuinely different disk revision still captures and clears `recoveryPhase` (`setRecoveryPhase(null)` alongside `updateExternalChange`), letting the ordinary observed banner supersede a stale `failed`/`reloading` one. `attemptRecoveryWrite` gained an `issuedEpoch` guard so an in-flight attempt whose epoch is superseded by a fresh capture never overwrites that capture's own phase with a stale reloading/pending/failed transition. An earlier, simpler attempt — advancing `diskContentRef` itself to the captured bytes on Reload click — was tried and rejected: it reddened "Reload recovers the captured disk bytes after an already-issued save publishes (#1599)", because `diskContentRef` means "what Emend last confirmed IS on disk" (moved only by a real read or a real write's own completion) and detection-bound v1 permits an already-issued save from *before* detection to complete with *different* bytes after capture — advancing it on a mere click asserts evidence that does not yet exist. Three new tests pin the fixed axis directly: a genuinely new divergence during `failed` supersedes to the ordinary banner: Try again disappears; the same captured bytes re-read repeatedly once a recovery attempt has failed never resurrect the ordinary banner over the failed one — probed after failure specifically, since the existing `ownPublication` (#1599) mechanism already covers the same-bytes case while a write is genuinely in flight, so only the post-failure window isolates `dismissedDiskContentRef`'s own contribution; and a new divergence captured while a retry is already in flight leaves `onDirtyChange` never called with `false` once that stale retry lands. **Important 1:** the primary Ctrl/Cmd+S path (`manualSave`) moved focus to `Keep mine` but never called `setSaveBlocked(true)`, so AC3's rewritten two lines never appeared on the keyboard path a screen-reader author actually uses — only the imperative dirty-close-guard `save()` set it. Fixed by adding the same call or the manual path; pinned by "Keep mine is the highlighted default…"'s sibling `resets the blocked-Save copy…` case (which exercises `ref.current?.save()`, the same imperative seam) plus manual re-verification via `manualSave`'s own Ctrl+S keybinding path. **Important 2:** a failed `Try again` overwrote `failed.diskContent` with the *submitted* buffer instead of keeping the real disk-side bytes, so Compare's `On disk` pane showed the author's own edits after a retry. Fixed by threading `diskSide` through `attemptRecoveryWrite` separately from `content` — carried forward from the existing `failed` state on a retry rather than re-derived from what is being submitted. **Important 3 (T6):** the banner's own bar chrome was exact but its buttons were reading the generic shared `--color-accent`/`--color-border`/`--color-bg` tokens instead of `document-integrity.md`'s "Buttons" table; fixed with existing exact-hex tokens already in `styles.css` (`--color-action-border`, `--color-session-dialog-bg`/`-border`, `--color-tab-accent`), scoped to the `--external-change` modifier. **Minor (1 of 3, mutation-proven):** `setSaveBlocked(false)` inside `updateExternalChange` had no dedicated test (269/269 survived its removal); the new `resets the blocked-Save copy…` case pins it directly and reddens under the same removal. The other two Minors (a stray lower-cased/ASCII-apostrophe quote of the failed heading in `ux-design.md`; `:focus-visible`/`:disabled` added to the shared `.integrity-banner__action` base instead of the `--external-change` modifier, incidentally restyling `CrlfBanner`/`MissingOnDiskBanner`) are corrected in place with no new test surface. **Review round 3 (2 Important, addressed; PR #2108):** round 2's own fixes had shipped with no regression test of their own — `CLAUDE.md`'s TDD/pin-every-bugfix rule applied to them the same way it did to the Critical, and mutation testing confirmed both survived: dropping `setSaveBlocked(true)` from `manualSave` (the Ctrl/Cmd+S path) left all 39 `src/editor/` test files green, since every existing blocked-copy assertion drove the choice through the imperative `save()` handle, never the real keybinding; reverting `diskContent: diskSide` to `: content` in `attemptRecoveryWrite`'s failure branch also left the suite green, since no assertion covered Compare after a failed retry with further local edits. New tests close both: `manual Save (Ctrl/Cmd+S) rewrites both banner lines to the exact blocked-Save copy, not only the focus move (AC3, review Important 2, PR #2108)` drives the real keybinding (`fireEvent.keyDown` with the platform-correct modifier, mirroring the existing `#1599` Ctrl/Cmd+S test) and asserts both ratified lines, not just the pre-existing focus-only check; `Compare's On disk pane shows the real disk side, not the submitted buffer, after a failed retry with further edits (review Important 2, PR #2108)` types past the original Reload failure, retries and fails again, and asserts the two Compare panes independently (`within(region).getByText("On disk").closest("section")`) rather than checking the region's combined text, which the original round-1 `Compare still works from the failed state…` case could not have caught even with `diskSide` reverted, since both panes would have shown the same unedited text at that point in its own scenario. Also fixed: `styles.css`'s highlighted-default button hardcoded `color: #ffffff` instead of the file's own paired `--color-accent-contrast` idiom — measured in real Chromium under `data-theme="dark"`, white on the dark-theme accent is 3.14:1 (fails AA for 11.5px text) where the token itself resolves to `#10131a` at 6.01:1; light theme is unaffected either way since the token is `#ffffff` there too. |

activity feed is the observability surface. P2a integration (#412): the FR-AGENT-7 queue-visibility half lands — `commands::agent::run_state` makes a run's phase and queue position pollable, and `agent::session::KillHandle` lets `stop` cancel a run whose stream is being drained on a background thread without contending with it. #807 moves prompt delivery onto a dedicated writer so pipe backpressure cannot hold the run-queue lock and the Stop handle exists before stdin drains; an early provider exit deterministically records the prompt-write failure rather than a false `Completed`. Review pass (PR #452): `RunQueueState::last_finished` closes the gap where a run's terminal phase was unobservable whenever `drain_and_advance` overwrote `active` with a queued successor in the same lock acquisition. **P2a integration, activity-streaming half (#345):** `RunStatus` gains `activity`/`elapsed_secs` (frozen at whatever it reached when the run resolved, never drifting on a later poll) and `RunPhase::Stopped` gains `delivered` (ux-design.md §2/J7's "nothing applied" vs "partial delivery", read off `AgentSession::saw_propose_changes` at kill time — **narrowed by #1404 below**, which widens this to `saw_delivery` so the comment lane reports honestly too); a new `agent::run_queue::ActivityEmitter` seam (`NoopEmitter` by default, every existing test unaffected) is pushed a fresh `RunStatus` on every new activity line and on finalize — `commands::agent::TauriActivityEmitter` bridges it onto a real `AppHandle::emit` on the `agent://activity` channel (`src-tauri/src/lib.rs`'s `.setup()`, this app's first `emit()`/`listen()` pair, P2a-21 journal). `commands::agent::start_run_on` is the `State`-extractor-free seam both `start_run` and the D23 driver share. The D23 `crates/test-driver` gets `/start_run`/`/stop_run`/`/run_state` (via `serve_with_queue`, an injectable-`RunQueue` seam split out of `serve` — see its own docs) — the acceptance criteria's required driver-observability half, HTTP poll rather than a push (no SSE/websocket; `#345`'s own PR states this transport trade-off; `/run_state` carries the identical `RunStatus` shape either way). Frontend half: see the dedicated `FR-X-3` vitest row below. Review pass (PR #477 round 1): the freeze-once-terminal half of `elapsed_secs` had zero real coverage — the integration-level test polled a `last_finished` snapshot twice, which is equal by construction regardless of whether anything is frozen. Narrowed that test to what it can actually observe (`elapsed_secs_is_present_and_computable_while_a_run_is_active`) and pinned the freeze property itself at the unit level instead, where a fabricated `ActiveRun` can plant `started_at`/`finished_at` far enough from real "now" to distinguish a frozen reading from a live one (`elapsed_secs_is_frozen_at_finished_at_not_the_live_wall_clock` / `elapsed_secs_tracks_the_live_wall_clock_while_still_running`) — confirmed by mutation (reverting the freeze in `ActiveRun::status` fails the new test). Also fixed: `spawn_and_track`'s `ActivityEmitter::emit` was called while `RunQueue`'s own lock was still held at both call sites (`start`, `drain_and_advance`), contradicting the trait's own "never called while the lock is held" doc invariant; `spawn_and_track` now returns the `RunStatus` instead of emitting it, and both callers emit only after dropping the guard. **#1149 fix:** `serve()` (`crates/test-driver`) used to wire the real `spawn_claude_run` into its `RunQueue` unguarded; before #1206, `EMEND_CLAUDE_BIN` redirected only `agent::providers`' detection scan and never this spawn path, so any e2e POSTing `/start_run` on a box with the real `claude` on `PATH` could spawn and bill it. `guarded_spawn_claude_run` now refuses before ever building an invocation unless the never-set-by-CI `EMEND_TEST_DRIVER_ALLOW_REAL_CLAUDE_SPAWN` escape hatch carries the exact value `1` — fail-closed, so an unset var (or the `=0` a reader would reach for to turn the hatch *off*) defaults safe rather than to billing. `start_run_through_serve_never_spawns_the_real_claude_cli_even_when_one_is_genuinely_on_path` drives this through the real `serve()` path with a runnable fake planted as `claude` on the child driver's `PATH` and an open project, proving the guard holds under the full hazard precondition. #1206 does not bypass that guard: only after the exact-value hatch permits delegation does the shared Claude resolver make `EMEND_CLAUDE_BIN` select the executable at the production spawn boundary. **#1404 fix:** `drain_and_advance`'s `delivered` used to read `AgentSession::saw_propose_changes` alone, so a run stopped after it had already announced an `add_comments`/`reply_to_comment` call — durably on the TD-4 sidecar — still resolved `Stopped { delivered: false }` and rendered "STOPPED — nothing applied", the same dishonest stop card J7's two-outcome split exists to prevent (FR-X-5). `stopping_a_run_after_an_add_comments_announcement_resolves_delivered_true` pins the comment-lane case against `saw_delivery` (#1118's wider TD-1 §3.2 delivery signal), sibling to the existing `stopping_a_run_after_a_propose_changes_announcement_resolves_delivered_true` for tracked changes. #2329: the four cells that assert on the injected emitter now wait on the emitter itself (`wait_until_emitter_terminal`) rather than on the queue's phase. The run thread writes the terminal phase under the queue lock, drops it, and only then pushes — because `ActivityEmitter` must not be called while that lock is held — so `wait_until_terminal` returning proves nothing about the push, and the loaded Windows runner lost that window intermittently on the required gate. The helper returns the snapshot it waited on, so the assertions read exactly what was observed instead of re-reading. |

| FR-X-3, FR-X-5, FR-AGENT-6 | A spawned agent process that exits unsuccessfully is reported through J7's existing Failed run phase with its stderr diagnostic, while successful stderr chatter remains harmless and bounded | rust | a_fatal_child_exit_reports_its_stderr_as_a_failed_run, a_successful_run_with_noisy_stderr_still_completes, a_noisy_stderr_never_blocks_draining_stdout, stderr_capture_retains_only_the_newest_bounded_tail_across_chunks, stderr_redactions_remove_inline_mcp_authorization_values_and_bare_tokens, a_clean_child_that_exits_after_stdout_eof_still_completes, stop_remains_live_while_a_child_lingers_after_stdout_eof, reap_contains_a_descendant_that_keeps_only_stderr_open, reap_finishes_when_stderr_outlives_the_process_group, finishing_stderr_collection_is_bounded_even_with_continuous_output, stderr_finish_is_bounded_with_a_real_open_empty_pipe, stderr_drop_is_bounded_with_a_real_open_empty_pipe, stderr_finish_retains_buffered_bytes_from_a_real_open_pipe, delayed_exit_fixture_closes_stdout_before_its_process_exits | present | #2395: `AgentSession` drains stderr concurrently on a dedicated thread and retains only its newest 16 KiB, so a full stderr pipe cannot block stdout and unbounded diagnostics cannot grow memory without limit. Before the failed run reason leaves the backend, values from sensitive fields in inline JSON arguments are removed, including the MCP `Authorization` header and its bare bearer token. Once the process leader's status is captured, group cleanup is requested, and bounded stderr collection finishes, `SessionExit` distinguishes a successful exit (stderr ignored) from a nonzero exit and carries the bounded diagnostic through `RunQueue`'s existing `Failed { message }` channel. The fatal cell spawns a real OS child whose spawn succeeds before it writes stderr and exits 23; the success near-match drives fake-agent's existing 4 MiB noisy-stderr mode through the queue. The post-EOF cells use a shared Rust child fixture on both platforms, with a direct pipe-read test proving actual stdout EOF while the child remains alive. A scope guard stops the fixture on test failure as well as success. These cells separate stdout closure from process-group exit: normal reaping observes the leader's own clean status and requests group cleanup. A nonblocking stderr reader drains available diagnostics for at most another 100 ms on finish, so even a surviving or continuously writing descriptor cannot strand its join; dropping its owner also requests finish and joins the reader. The deterministic inherited-descriptor fixture creates a separate process group and blocks on a FIFO. Three platform-independent cells also spawn a real Rust child that keeps stderr open while waiting on stdin: finish and Drop each have a two-second failure bound with no available bytes, and finish retains diagnostics buffered before the reader starts. These cells execute the native pipe adapter in the full Windows Rust suite. Stop can still acquire the process-group handle and interrupt the bounded wait. Stop wins as `Stopped`, and provider-reported/read failures retain their existing precedence. |

Re-derived (PR #2271 review round 1, which found the first figures were a stale run's) by running the FINAL test files against UNCHANGED base production code at `9a4853f6d` in a scratch worktree. The three RENDERING files — the only ones whose result speaks to a rendered string — are **68 tests, 66 identical, 2 red**: `ProjectRail.test.tsx` 21/21 green, `TimelinePanel.startRun.test.tsx` 16/16 green (identity proven at those two call sites), `TimelinePanel.batchAddress.test.tsx` 29 green / 2 red, both reds the `CommandError` cases at `:818` and `:851` rendering `[object Object]`. `backend.test.ts` is counted SEPARATELY at 10 tests / 1 red, because its red is the new `fallback` parameter's own unit test — a compile-level absence of a new argument, never a rendering difference; folding it into the rendering total is what made the first published figures (72/69/3) irreproducible. The `ProjectRail` cases pin identity by construction — its fallback branch always yields `kind: "UpdateFailed"`, and that banner renders the fixed `Couldn’t update files` with no detail at all, so `ProjectFilesError.message` reaches no surface on that branch (its value does change for a bare-string or duck-typed rejection; nothing renders it). Axis varied at every site: the rejection's SHAPE (typed CommandError, thrown `Error`, bare string, duck-typed `{message}`, message-less object, `undefined`). Axis held fixed: the call site — varied instead by driving each surface's own production composition (the real composer/trigger/rail through the real `startRun`/`refresh` with only the Tauri boundary mocked). `src/backend.ts`, `src/backend.test.ts`, `src/projectRail/ProjectRail.tsx`, `src/projectRail/ProjectRail.test.tsx`, `src/timeline/agentActivity.ts`, `src/timeline/TimelinePanel.tsx`, `src/timeline/TimelinePanel.startRun.test.tsx`, `src/timeline/TimelinePanel.batchAddress.test.tsx`. #2328's review found the same defect class one layer out from #2327: a refused window close reached the author as “Emend couldn’t complete the document action”, naming a subject that was never acted on. | NI-windows-host-cohort | The isolation canary and contention benchmark each require one selected host label, publish every slot's actual Windows hostname, and compare those hostnames before accepting a cohort. Labels make co-location requestable; hostname equality is the independent backstop against a mislabelled runner. Missing, duplicate, malformed, or unequal hostname evidence fails closed. A canary hostname mismatch publishes its existing neutral NOT MEASURED verdict and keeps the workspace probe closed; a benchmark mismatch fails before sampling. Pre-fix benchmark run `32731223148` assigned runner 28 (`cowrite-windows-4`, FINN-PC10) and runner 22 (`cowrite-windows`, WILLIACJ-01) in the same second; both existing cohort validators passed before the run was cancelled, proving the benchmark defect. Live host labels were then applied through the runner API: ids 22/24 carry `host-williacj-01`, and ids 27/28 carry `host-finn-pc10`. Post-fix exact-head canary run `32733419997` convened runners 27/28 on FINN-PC10 and passed both hostname backstops plus both workspace probes; benchmark run `32736736710` used the same two runners and hostname, then passed both overlap guards, both hostname guards, both complete suites, and both whole-window sampler reports.
